1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→Memory Tool Module - Persistent Curated Memory
     4→
     5→Provides bounded, file-backed memory that persists across sessions. Two stores:
     6→  - MEMORY.md: agent's personal notes and observations (environment facts, project
     7→    conventions, tool quirks, things learned)
     8→  - USER.md: what the agent knows about the user (preferences, communication style,
     9→    expectations, workflow habits)
    10→
    11→Both are injected into the system prompt as a frozen snapshot at session start.
    12→Mid-session writes update files on disk immediately (durable) but do NOT change
    13→the system prompt -- this preserves the prefix cache for the entire session.
    14→The snapshot refreshes on the next session start.
    15→
    16→Entry delimiter: § (section sign). Entries can be multiline.
    17→Character limits (not tokens) because char counts are model-independent.
    18→
    19→Design:
    20→- Single `memory` tool with action parameter: add, replace, remove, read
    21→- replace/remove use short unique substring matching (not full text or IDs)
    22→- Behavioral guidance lives in the tool schema description
    23→- Frozen snapshot pattern: system prompt is stable, tool responses show live state
    24→"""
    25→
    26→import json
    27→import logging
    28→import os
    29→import re
    30→import tempfile
    31→from pathlib import Path
    32→from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional
    33→
    34→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    35→
    36→# Where memory files live
    37→MEMORY_DIR = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes")) / "memories"
    38→
    39→ENTRY_DELIMITER = "\n§\n"
    40→
    41→
    42→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    43→# Memory content scanning — lightweight check for injection/exfiltration
    44→# in content that gets injected into the system prompt.
    45→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    46→
    47→_MEMORY_THREAT_PATTERNS = [
    48→    # Prompt injection
    49→    (r'ignore\s+(previous|all|above|prior)\s+instructions', "prompt_injection"),
    50→    (r'you\s+are\s+now\s+', "role_hijack"),
    51→    (r'do\s+not\s+tell\s+the\s+user', "deception_hide"),
    52→    (r'system\s+prompt\s+override', "sys_prompt_override"),
    53→    (r'disregard\s+(your|all|any)\s+(instructions|rules|guidelines)', "disregard_rules"),
    54→    (r'act\s+as\s+(if|though)\s+you\s+(have\s+no|don\'t\s+have)\s+(restrictions|limits|rules)', "bypass_restrictions"),
    55→    # Exfiltration via curl/wget with secrets
    56→    (r'curl\s+[^\n]*\$\{?\w*(KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL|API)', "exfil_curl"),
    57→    (r'wget\s+[^\n]*\$\{?\w*(KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL|API)', "exfil_wget"),
    58→    (r'cat\s+[^\n]*(\.env|credentials|\.netrc|\.pgpass|\.npmrc|\.pypirc)', "read_secrets"),
    59→    # Persistence via shell rc
    60→    (r'authorized_keys', "ssh_backdoor"),
    61→    (r'\$HOME/\.ssh|\~/\.ssh', "ssh_access"),
    62→    (r'\$HOME/\.hermes/\.env|\~/\.hermes/\.env', "hermes_env"),
    63→]
    64→
    65→# Subset of invisible chars for injection detection
    66→_INVISIBLE_CHARS = {
    67→    '\u200b', '\u200c', '\u200d', '\u2060', '\ufeff',
    68→    '\u202a', '\u202b', '\u202c', '\u202d', '\u202e',
    69→}
    70→
    71→
    72→def _scan_memory_content(content: str) -> Optional[str]:
    73→    """Scan memory content for injection/exfil patterns. Returns error string if blocked."""
    74→    # Check invisible unicode
    75→    for char in _INVISIBLE_CHARS:
    76→        if char in content:
    77→            return f"Blocked: content contains invisible unicode character U+{ord(char):04X} (possible injection)."
    78→
    79→    # Check threat patterns
    80→    for pattern, pid in _MEMORY_THREAT_PATTERNS:
    81→        if re.search(pattern, content, re.IGNORECASE):
    82→            return f"Blocked: content matches threat pattern '{pid}'. Memory entries are injected into the system prompt and must not contain injection or exfiltration payloads."
    83→
    84→    return None
    85→
    86→
    87→class MemoryStore:
    88→    """
    89→    Bounded curated memory with file persistence. One instance per AIAgent.
    90→
    91→    Maintains two parallel states:
    92→      - _system_prompt_snapshot: frozen at load time, used for system prompt injection.
    93→        Never mutated mid-session. Keeps prefix cache stable.
    94→      - memory_entries / user_entries: live state, mutated by tool calls, persisted to disk.
    95→        Tool responses always reflect this live state.
    96→    """
    97→
    98→    def __init__(self, memory_char_limit: int = 2200, user_char_limit: int = 1375):
    99→        self.memory_entries: List[str] = []
   100→        self.user_entries: List[str] = []
   101→        self.memory_char_limit = memory_char_limit
   102→        self.user_char_limit = user_char_limit
   103→        # Frozen snapshot for system prompt -- set once at load_from_disk()
   104→        self._system_prompt_snapshot: Dict[str, str] = {"memory": "", "user": ""}
   105→
   106→    def load_from_disk(self):
   107→        """Load entries from MEMORY.md and USER.md, capture system prompt snapshot."""
   108→        MEMORY_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   109→
   110→        self.memory_entries = self._read_file(MEMORY_DIR / "MEMORY.md")
   111→        self.user_entries = self._read_file(MEMORY_DIR / "USER.md")
   112→
   113→        # Deduplicate entries (preserves order, keeps first occurrence)
   114→        self.memory_entries = list(dict.fromkeys(self.memory_entries))
   115→        self.user_entries = list(dict.fromkeys(self.user_entries))
   116→
   117→        # Capture frozen snapshot for system prompt injection
   118→        self._system_prompt_snapshot = {
   119→            "memory": self._render_block("memory", self.memory_entries),
   120→            "user": self._render_block("user", self.user_entries),
   121→        }
   122→
   123→    def save_to_disk(self, target: str):
   124→        """Persist entries to the appropriate file. Called after every mutation."""
   125→        MEMORY_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   126→
   127→        if target == "memory":
   128→            self._write_file(MEMORY_DIR / "MEMORY.md", self.memory_entries)
   129→        elif target == "user":
   130→            self._write_file(MEMORY_DIR / "USER.md", self.user_entries)
   131→
   132→    def _entries_for(self, target: str) -> List[str]:
   133→        if target == "user":
   134→            return self.user_entries
   135→        return self.memory_entries
   136→
   137→    def _set_entries(self, target: str, entries: List[str]):
   138→        if target == "user":
   139→            self.user_entries = entries
   140→        else:
   141→            self.memory_entries = entries
   142→
   143→    def _char_count(self, target: str) -> int:
   144→        entries = self._entries_for(target)
   145→        if not entries:
   146→            return 0
   147→        return len(ENTRY_DELIMITER.join(entries))
   148→
   149→    def _char_limit(self, target: str) -> int:
   150→        if target == "user":
   151→            return self.user_char_limit
   152→        return self.memory_char_limit
   153→
   154→    def add(self, target: str, content: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   155→        """Append a new entry. Returns error if it would exceed the char limit."""
   156→        content = content.strip()
   157→        if not content:
   158→            return {"success": False, "error": "Content cannot be empty."}
   159→
   160→        # Scan for injection/exfiltration before accepting
   161→        scan_error = _scan_memory_content(content)
   162→        if scan_error:
   163→            return {"success": False, "error": scan_error}
   164→
   165→        entries = self._entries_for(target)
   166→        limit = self._char_limit(target)
   167→
   168→        # Reject exact duplicates
   169→        if content in entries:
   170→            return self._success_response(target, "Entry already exists (no duplicate added).")
   171→
   172→        # Calculate what the new total would be
   173→        new_entries = entries + [content]
   174→        new_total = len(ENTRY_DELIMITER.join(new_entries))
   175→
   176→        if new_total > limit:
   177→            current = self._char_count(target)
   178→            return {
   179→                "success": False,
   180→                "error": (
   181→                    f"Memory at {current:,}/{limit:,} chars. "
   182→                    f"Adding this entry ({len(content)} chars) would exceed the limit. "
   183→                    f"Replace or remove existing entries first."
   184→                ),
   185→                "current_entries": entries,
   186→                "usage": f"{current:,}/{limit:,}",
   187→            }
   188→
   189→        entries.append(content)
   190→        self._set_entries(target, entries)
   191→        self.save_to_disk(target)
   192→
   193→        return self._success_response(target, "Entry added.")
   194→
   195→    def replace(self, target: str, old_text: str, new_content: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   196→        """Find entry containing old_text substring, replace it with new_content."""
   197→        old_text = old_text.strip()
   198→        new_content = new_content.strip()
   199→        if not old_text:
   200→            return {"success": False, "error": "old_text cannot be empty."}
   201→        if not new_content:
   202→            return {"success": False, "error": "new_content cannot be empty. Use 'remove' to delete entries."}
   203→
   204→        # Scan replacement content for injection/exfiltration
   205→        scan_error = _scan_memory_content(new_content)
   206→        if scan_error:
   207→            return {"success": False, "error": scan_error}
   208→
   209→        entries = self._entries_for(target)
   210→        matches = [(i, e) for i, e in enumerate(entries) if old_text in e]
   211→
   212→        if len(matches) == 0:
   213→            return {"success": False, "error": f"No entry matched '{old_text}'."}
   214→
   215→        if len(matches) > 1:
   216→            # If all matches are identical (exact duplicates), operate on the first one
   217→            unique_texts = set(e for _, e in matches)
   218→            if len(unique_texts) > 1:
   219→                previews = [e[:80] + ("..." if len(e) > 80 else "") for _, e in matches]
   220→                return {
   221→                    "success": False,
   222→                    "error": f"Multiple entries matched '{old_text}'. Be more specific.",
   223→                    "matches": previews,
   224→                }
   225→            # All identical -- safe to replace just the first
   226→
   227→        idx = matches[0][0]
   228→        limit = self._char_limit(target)
   229→
   230→        # Check that replacement doesn't blow the budget
   231→        test_entries = entries.copy()
   232→        test_entries[idx] = new_content
   233→        new_total = len(ENTRY_DELIMITER.join(test_entries))
   234→
   235→        if new_total > limit:
   236→            return {
   237→                "success": False,
   238→                "error": (
   239→                    f"Replacement would put memory at {new_total:,}/{limit:,} chars. "
   240→                    f"Shorten the new content or remove other entries first."
   241→                ),
   242→            }
   243→
   244→        entries[idx] = new_content
   245→        self._set_entries(target, entries)
   246→        self.save_to_disk(target)
   247→
   248→        return self._success_response(target, "Entry replaced.")
   249→
   250→    def remove(self, target: str, old_text: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   251→        """Remove the entry containing old_text substring."""
   252→        old_text = old_text.strip()
   253→        if not old_text:
   254→            return {"success": False, "error": "old_text cannot be empty."}
   255→
   256→        entries = self._entries_for(target)
   257→        matches = [(i, e) for i, e in enumerate(entries) if old_text in e]
   258→
   259→        if len(matches) == 0:
   260→            return {"success": False, "error": f"No entry matched '{old_text}'."}
   261→
   262→        if len(matches) > 1:
   263→            # If all matches are identical (exact duplicates), remove the first one
   264→            unique_texts = set(e for _, e in matches)
   265→            if len(unique_texts) > 1:
   266→                previews = [e[:80] + ("..." if len(e) > 80 else "") for _, e in matches]
   267→                return {
   268→                    "success": False,
   269→                    "error": f"Multiple entries matched '{old_text}'. Be more specific.",
   270→                    "matches": previews,
   271→                }
   272→            # All identical -- safe to remove just the first
   273→
   274→        idx = matches[0][0]
   275→        entries.pop(idx)
   276→        self._set_entries(target, entries)
   277→        self.save_to_disk(target)
   278→
   279→        return self._success_response(target, "Entry removed.")
   280→
   281→    def format_for_system_prompt(self, target: str) -> Optional[str]:
   282→        """
   283→        Return the frozen snapshot for system prompt injection.
   284→
   285→        This returns the state captured at load_from_disk() time, NOT the live
   286→        state. Mid-session writes do not affect this. This keeps the system
   287→        prompt stable across all turns, preserving the prefix cache.
   288→
   289→        Returns None if the snapshot is empty (no entries at load time).
   290→        """
   291→        block = self._system_prompt_snapshot.get(target, "")
   292→        return block if block else None
   293→
   294→    # -- Internal helpers --
   295→
   296→    def _success_response(self, target: str, message: str = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   297→        entries = self._entries_for(target)
   298→        current = self._char_count(target)
   299→        limit = self._char_limit(target)
   300→        pct = int((current / limit) * 100) if limit > 0 else 0
   301→
   302→        resp = {
   303→            "success": True,
   304→            "target": target,
   305→            "entries": entries,
   306→            "usage": f"{pct}% — {current:,}/{limit:,} chars",
   307→            "entry_count": len(entries),
   308→        }
   309→        if message:
   310→            resp["message"] = message
   311→        return resp
   312→
   313→    def _render_block(self, target: str, entries: List[str]) -> str:
   314→        """Render a system prompt block with header and usage indicator."""
   315→        if not entries:
   316→            return ""
   317→
   318→        limit = self._char_limit(target)
   319→        content = ENTRY_DELIMITER.join(entries)
   320→        current = len(content)
   321→        pct = int((current / limit) * 100) if limit > 0 else 0
   322→
   323→        if target == "user":
   324→            header = f"USER PROFILE (who the user is) [{pct}% — {current:,}/{limit:,} chars]"
   325→        else:
   326→            header = f"MEMORY (your personal notes) [{pct}% — {current:,}/{limit:,} chars]"
   327→
   328→        separator = "═" * 46
   329→        return f"{separator}\n{header}\n{separator}\n{content}"
   330→
   331→    @staticmethod
   332→    def _read_file(path: Path) -> List[str]:
   333→        """Read a memory file and split into entries.
   334→
   335→        No file locking needed: _write_file uses atomic rename, so readers
   336→        always see either the previous complete file or the new complete file.
   337→        """
   338→        if not path.exists():
   339→            return []
   340→        try:
   341→            raw = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   342→        except (OSError, IOError):
   343→            return []
   344→
   345→        if not raw.strip():
   346→            return []
   347→
   348→        # Use ENTRY_DELIMITER for consistency with _write_file. Splitting by "§"
   349→        # alone would incorrectly split entries that contain "§" in their content.
   350→        entries = [e.strip() for e in raw.split(ENTRY_DELIMITER)]
   351→        return [e for e in entries if e]
   352→
   353→    @staticmethod
   354→    def _write_file(path: Path, entries: List[str]):
   355→        """Write entries to a memory file using atomic temp-file + rename.
   356→
   357→        Previous implementation used open("w") + flock, but "w" truncates the
   358→        file *before* the lock is acquired, creating a race window where
   359→        concurrent readers see an empty file. Atomic rename avoids this:
   360→        readers always see either the old complete file or the new one.
   361→        """
   362→        content = ENTRY_DELIMITER.join(entries) if entries else ""
   363→        try:
   364→            # Write to temp file in same directory (same filesystem for atomic rename)
   365→            fd, tmp_path = tempfile.mkstemp(
   366→                dir=str(path.parent), suffix=".tmp", prefix=".mem_"
   367→            )
   368→            try:
   369→                with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   370→                    f.write(content)
   371→                    f.flush()
   372→                    os.fsync(f.fileno())
   373→                os.replace(tmp_path, str(path))  # Atomic on same filesystem
   374→            except BaseException:
   375→                # Clean up temp file on any failure
   376→                try:
   377→                    os.unlink(tmp_path)
   378→                except OSError:
   379→                    pass
   380→                raise
   381→        except (OSError, IOError) as e:
   382→            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to write memory file {path}: {e}")
   383→
   384→
   385→def memory_tool(
   386→    action: str,
   387→    target: str = "memory",
   388→    content: str = None,
   389→    old_text: str = None,
   390→    store: Optional[MemoryStore] = None,
   391→) -> str:
   392→    """
   393→    Single entry point for the memory tool. Dispatches to MemoryStore methods.
   394→
   395→    Returns JSON string with results.
   396→    """
   397→    if store is None:
   398→        return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": "Memory is not available. It may be disabled in config or this environment."}, ensure_ascii=False)
   399→
   400→    if target not in ("memory", "user"):
   401→        return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": f"Invalid target '{target}'. Use 'memory' or 'user'."}, ensure_ascii=False)
   402→
   403→    if action == "add":
   404→        if not content:
   405→            return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": "Content is required for 'add' action."}, ensure_ascii=False)
   406→        result = store.add(target, content)
   407→
   408→    elif action == "replace":
   409→        if not old_text:
   410→            return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": "old_text is required for 'replace' action."}, ensure_ascii=False)
   411→        if not content:
   412→            return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": "content is required for 'replace' action."}, ensure_ascii=False)
   413→        result = store.replace(target, old_text, content)
   414→
   415→    elif action == "remove":
   416→        if not old_text:
   417→            return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": "old_text is required for 'remove' action."}, ensure_ascii=False)
   418→        result = store.remove(target, old_text)
   419→
   420→    else:
   421→        return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": f"Unknown action '{action}'. Use: add, replace, remove"}, ensure_ascii=False)
   422→
   423→    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)
   424→
   425→
   426→def check_memory_requirements() -> bool:
   427→    """Memory tool has no external requirements -- always available."""
   428→    return True
   429→
   430→
   431→# =============================================================================
   432→# OpenAI Function-Calling Schema
   433→# =============================================================================
   434→
   435→MEMORY_SCHEMA = {
   436→    "name": "memory",
   437→    "description": (
   438→        "Save important information to persistent memory that survives across sessions. "
   439→        "Your memory appears in your system prompt at session start -- it's how you "
   440→        "remember things about the user and your environment between conversations.\n\n"
   441→        "WHEN TO SAVE (do this proactively, don't wait to be asked):\n"
   442→        "- User shares a preference, habit, or personal detail (name, role, timezone, coding style)\n"
   443→        "- You discover something about the environment (OS, installed tools, project structure)\n"
   444→        "- User corrects you or says 'remember this' / 'don't do that again'\n"
   445→        "- You learn a convention, API quirk, or workflow specific to this user's setup\n"
   446→        "- You completed something - log it like a diary entry\n"
   447→        "- After completing a complex task, save a brief note about what was done\n\n"
   448→        "- If you've discovered a new way to do something, solved a problem that could be necessary later, save it as a skill with the skill tool\n\n"
   449→        "TWO TARGETS:\n"
   450→        "- 'user': who the user is -- name, role, preferences, communication style, pet peeves\n"
   451→        "- 'memory': your notes -- environment facts, project conventions, tool quirks, lessons learned\n\n"
   452→        "ACTIONS: add (new entry), replace (update existing -- old_text identifies it), "
   453→        "remove (delete -- old_text identifies it).\n"
   454→        "Capacity shown in system prompt. When >80%, consolidate entries before adding new ones.\n\n"
   455→        "SKIP: trivial/obvious info, things easily re-discovered, raw data dumps."
   456→    ),
   457→    "parameters": {
   458→        "type": "object",
   459→        "properties": {
   460→            "action": {
   461→                "type": "string",
   462→                "enum": ["add", "replace", "remove"],
   463→                "description": "The action to perform."
   464→            },
   465→            "target": {
   466→                "type": "string",
   467→                "enum": ["memory", "user"],
   468→                "description": "Which memory store: 'memory' for personal notes, 'user' for user profile."
   469→            },
   470→            "content": {
   471→                "type": "string",
   472→                "description": "The entry content. Required for 'add' and 'replace'."
   473→            },
   474→            "old_text": {
   475→                "type": "string",
   476→                "description": "Short unique substring identifying the entry to replace or remove."
   477→            },
   478→        },
   479→        "required": ["action", "target"],
   480→    },
   481→}
   482→
   483→
   484→# --- Registry ---
   485→from tools.registry import registry
   486→
   487→registry.register(
   488→    name="memory",
   489→    toolset="memory",
   490→    schema=MEMORY_SCHEMA,
   491→    handler=lambda args, **kw: memory_tool(
   492→        action=args.get("action", ""),
   493→        target=args.get("target", "memory"),
   494→        content=args.get("content"),
   495→        old_text=args.get("old_text"),
   496→        store=kw.get("store")),
   497→    check_fn=check_memory_requirements,
   498→)
   499→
   500→
   501→
   502→
   503→
```

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→Model Tools Module
     4→
     5→Thin orchestration layer over the tool registry. Each tool file in tools/
     6→self-registers its schema, handler, and metadata via tools.registry.register().
     7→This module triggers discovery (by importing all tool modules), then provides
     8→the public API that run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, and the RL
     9→environments consume.
    10→
    11→Public API (signatures preserved from the original 2,400-line version):
    12→    get_tool_definitions(enabled_toolsets, disabled_toolsets, quiet_mode) -> list
    13→    handle_function_call(function_name, function_args, task_id, user_task) -> str
    14→    TOOL_TO_TOOLSET_MAP: dict          (for batch_runner.py)
    15→    TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS: dict         (for cli.py, doctor.py)
    16→    get_all_tool_names() -> list
    17→    get_toolset_for_tool(name) -> str
    18→    get_available_toolsets() -> dict
    19→    check_toolset_requirements() -> dict
    20→    check_tool_availability(quiet) -> tuple
    21→"""
    22→
    23→import json
    24→import asyncio
    25→import os
    26→import logging
    27→from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional, Tuple
    28→
    29→from tools.registry import registry
    30→from toolsets import resolve_toolset, validate_toolset
    31→
    32→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    33→
    34→
    35→# =============================================================================
    36→# Async Bridging  (single source of truth -- used by registry.dispatch too)
    37→# =============================================================================
    38→
    39→def _run_async(coro):
    40→    """Run an async coroutine from a sync context.
    41→
    42→    If the current thread already has a running event loop (e.g., inside
    43→    the gateway's async stack or Atropos's event loop), we spin up a
    44→    disposable thread so asyncio.run() can create its own loop without
    45→    conflicting.
    46→
    47→    This is the single source of truth for sync->async bridging in tool
    48→    handlers. The RL paths (agent_loop.py, tool_context.py) also provide
    49→    outer thread-pool wrapping as defense-in-depth, but each handler is
    50→    self-protecting via this function.
    51→    """
    52→    try:
    53→        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
    54→    except RuntimeError:
    55→        loop = None
    56→
    57→    if loop and loop.is_running():
    58→        import concurrent.futures
    59→        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1) as pool:
    60→            future = pool.submit(asyncio.run, coro)
    61→            return future.result(timeout=300)
    62→    return asyncio.run(coro)
    63→
    64→
    65→# =============================================================================
    66→# Tool Discovery  (importing each module triggers its registry.register calls)
    67→# =============================================================================
    68→
    69→def _discover_tools():
    70→    """Import all tool modules to trigger their registry.register() calls.
    71→
    72→    Wrapped in a function so import errors in optional tools (e.g., fal_client
    73→    not installed) don't prevent the rest from loading.
    74→    """
    75→    _modules = [
    76→        "tools.web_tools",
    77→        "tools.terminal_tool",
    78→        "tools.file_tools",
    79→        "tools.vision_tools",
    80→        "tools.mixture_of_agents_tool",
    81→        "tools.image_generation_tool",
    82→        "tools.skills_tool",
    83→        "tools.skill_manager_tool",
    84→        "tools.browser_tool",
    85→        "tools.cronjob_tools",
    86→        "tools.rl_training_tool",
    87→        "tools.tts_tool",
    88→        "tools.todo_tool",

    95→        "tools.send_message_tool",
    96→        "tools.honcho_tools",
    97→        "tools.homeassistant_tool",
    98→    ]
    99→    import importlib
   100→    for mod_name in _modules:
   101→        try:
   102→            importlib.import_module(mod_name)
   103→        except Exception as e:
   104→            logger.debug("Could not import %s: %s", mod_name, e)
   105→

   122→
   123→TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS: Dict[str, dict] = registry.get_toolset_requirements()
   124→
   125→# Resolved tool names from the last get_tool_definitions() call.
   126→# Used by code_execution_tool to know which tools are available in this session.
   127→_last_resolved_tool_names: List[str] = []
   128→
   129→
   130→# =============================================================================
   131→# Legacy toolset name mapping  (old _tools-suffixed names -> tool name lists)
   132→# =============================================================================
   133→
   134→_LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP = {
   135→    "web_tools": ["web_search", "web_extract"],
   136→    "terminal_tools": ["terminal"],
   137→    "vision_tools": ["vision_analyze"],
   138→    "moa_tools": ["mixture_of_agents"],
   139→    "image_tools": ["image_generate"],

   141→    "browser_tools": [
   142→        "browser_navigate", "browser_snapshot", "browser_click",
   143→        "browser_type", "browser_scroll", "browser_back",
   144→        "browser_press", "browser_close", "browser_get_images",
   145→        "browser_vision"
   146→    ],
   147→    "cronjob_tools": ["schedule_cronjob", "list_cronjobs", "remove_cronjob"],
   148→    "rl_tools": [
   149→        "rl_list_environments", "rl_select_environment",
   150→        "rl_get_current_config", "rl_edit_config",
   151→        "rl_start_training", "rl_check_status",
   152→        "rl_stop_training", "rl_get_results",
   153→        "rl_list_runs", "rl_test_inference"
   154→    ],
   155→    "file_tools": ["read_file", "write_file", "patch", "search_files"],
   156→    "tts_tools": ["text_to_speech"],
   157→}
   158→
   159→
   160→# =============================================================================
   161→# get_tool_definitions  (the main schema provider)
   162→# =============================================================================
   163→
   164→def get_tool_definitions(
   165→    enabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   166→    disabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   167→    quiet_mode: bool = False,
   168→) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   169→    """
   170→    Get tool definitions for model API calls with toolset-based filtering.
   171→
   172→    All tools must be part of a toolset to be accessible.
   173→
   174→    Args:
   175→        enabled_toolsets: Only include tools from these toolsets.
   176→        disabled_toolsets: Exclude tools from these toolsets (if enabled_toolsets is None).
   177→        quiet_mode: Suppress status prints.
   178→
   179→    Returns:
   180→        Filtered list of OpenAI-format tool definitions.
   181→    """
   182→    # Determine which tool names the caller wants
   183→    tools_to_include: set = set()
   184→
   185→    if enabled_toolsets:
   186→        for toolset_name in enabled_toolsets:
   187→            if validate_toolset(toolset_name):
   188→                resolved = resolve_toolset(toolset_name)
   189→                tools_to_include.update(resolved)
   190→                if not quiet_mode:
   191→                    print(f"✅ Enabled toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(resolved) if resolved else 'no tools'}")
   192→            elif toolset_name in _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP:
   193→                legacy_tools = _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP[toolset_name]
   194→                tools_to_include.update(legacy_tools)
   195→                if not quiet_mode:
   196→                    print(f"✅ Enabled legacy toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(legacy_tools)}")
   197→            else:
   198→                if not quiet_mode:
   199→                    print(f"⚠️  Unknown toolset: {toolset_name}")
   200→
   201→    elif disabled_toolsets:
   202→        from toolsets import get_all_toolsets
   203→        for ts_name in get_all_toolsets():
   204→            tools_to_include.update(resolve_toolset(ts_name))
   205→
   206→        for toolset_name in disabled_toolsets:
   207→            if validate_toolset(toolset_name):
   208→                resolved = resolve_toolset(toolset_name)
   209→                tools_to_include.difference_update(resolved)
   210→                if not quiet_mode:
   211→                    print(f"🚫 Disabled toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(resolved) if resolved else 'no tools'}")
   212→            elif toolset_name in _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP:
   213→                legacy_tools = _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP[toolset_name]
   214→                tools_to_include.difference_update(legacy_tools)
   215→                if not quiet_mode:
   216→                    print(f"🚫 Disabled legacy toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(legacy_tools)}")
   217→            else:
   218→                if not quiet_mode:
   219→                    print(f"⚠️  Unknown toolset: {toolset_name}")
   220→    else:
   221→        from toolsets import get_all_toolsets
   222→        for ts_name in get_all_toolsets():
   223→            tools_to_include.update(resolve_toolset(ts_name))
   224→
   225→    # Ask the registry for schemas (only returns tools whose check_fn passes)
   226→    filtered_tools = registry.get_definitions(tools_to_include, quiet=quiet_mode)
   227→
   228→    # Rebuild execute_code schema to only list sandbox tools that are actually
   229→    # enabled.  Without this, the model sees "web_search is available in
   230→    # execute_code" even when the user disabled the web toolset (#560-discord).
   231→    if "execute_code" in tools_to_include:
   232→        from tools.code_execution_tool import SANDBOX_ALLOWED_TOOLS, build_execute_code_schema
   233→        sandbox_enabled = SANDBOX_ALLOWED_TOOLS & tools_to_include
   234→        dynamic_schema = build_execute_code_schema(sandbox_enabled)
   235→        for i, td in enumerate(filtered_tools):
   236→            if td.get("function", {}).get("name") == "execute_code":
   237→                filtered_tools[i] = {"type": "function", "function": dynamic_schema}
   238→                break
   239→
   240→    if not quiet_mode:
   241→        if filtered_tools:
   242→            tool_names = [t["function"]["name"] for t in filtered_tools]
   243→            print(f"🛠️  Final tool selection ({len(filtered_tools)} tools): {', '.join(tool_names)}")
   244→        else:
   245→            print("🛠️  No tools selected (all filtered out or unavailable)")
   246→
   247→    global _last_resolved_tool_names
   248→    _last_resolved_tool_names = [t["function"]["name"] for t in filtered_tools]
   249→
   250→    return filtered_tools
   251→
   252→
   253→# =============================================================================
   254→# handle_function_call  (the main dispatcher)
   255→# =============================================================================
   256→
   257→# Tools whose execution is intercepted by the agent loop (run_agent.py)
   258→# because they need agent-level state (TodoStore, MemoryStore, etc.).
   259→# The registry still holds their schemas; dispatch just returns a stub error
   260→# so if something slips through, the LLM sees a sensible message.
   261→_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS = {"todo", "memory", "session_search", "delegate_task"}
   262→
   263→
   264→def handle_function_call(
   265→    function_name: str,
   266→    function_args: Dict[str, Any],
   267→    task_id: Optional[str] = None,
   268→    user_task: Optional[str] = None,
   269→    enabled_tools: Optional[List[str]] = None,
   270→) -> str:
   271→    """
   272→    Main function call dispatcher that routes calls to the tool registry.
   273→
   274→    Args:
   275→        function_name: Name of the function to call.
   276→        function_args: Arguments for the function.
   277→        task_id: Unique identifier for terminal/browser session isolation.
   278→        user_task: The user's original task (for browser_snapshot context).
   279→        enabled_tools: Tool names enabled for this session.  When provided,
   280→                       execute_code uses this list to determine which sandbox
   281→                       tools to generate.  Falls back to the process-global
   282→                       ``_last_resolved_tool_names`` for backward compat.
   283→
   284→    Returns:
   285→        Function result as a JSON string.
   286→    """
   287→    # Notify the read-loop tracker when a non-read/search tool runs,
   288→    # so the *consecutive* counter resets (reads after other work are fine).
   289→    _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS = {"read_file", "search_files"}
   290→    if function_name not in _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS:
   291→        try:
   292→            from tools.file_tools import notify_other_tool_call
   293→            notify_other_tool_call(task_id or "default")
   294→        except Exception:
   295→            pass  # file_tools may not be loaded yet
   296→
   297→    try:
   298→        if function_name in _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS:
   299→            return json.dumps({"error": f"{function_name} must be handled by the agent loop"})
   300→
   301→        if function_name == "execute_code":
   302→            # Prefer the caller-provided list so subagents can't overwrite
   303→            # the parent's tool set via the process-global.
   304→            sandbox_enabled = enabled_tools if enabled_tools is not None else _last_resolved_tool_names
   305→            return registry.dispatch(
   306→                function_name, function_args,
   307→                task_id=task_id,
   308→                enabled_tools=sandbox_enabled,
   309→            )
   310→
   311→        return registry.dispatch(
   312→            function_name, function_args,
   313→            task_id=task_id,
   314→            user_task=user_task,
   315→        )
   316→
   317→    except Exception as e:
   318→        error_msg = f"Error executing {function_name}: {str(e)}"
   319→        logger.error(error_msg)
   320→        return json.dumps({"error": error_msg}, ensure_ascii=False)
   321→
   322→
   323→# =============================================================================
   324→# Backward-compat wrapper functions
   325→# =============================================================================
   326→
   327→def get_all_tool_names() -> List[str]:
   328→    """Return all registered tool names."""
   329→    return registry.get_all_tool_names()
   330→
   331→
   332→def get_toolset_for_tool(tool_name: str) -> Optional[str]:
   333→    """Return the toolset a tool belongs to."""
   334→    return registry.get_toolset_for_tool(tool_name)
   335→
   336→
   337→def get_available_toolsets() -> Dict[str, dict]:
   338→    """Return toolset availability info for UI display."""
   339→    return registry.get_available_toolsets()
   340→
   341→
   342→def check_toolset_requirements() -> Dict[str, bool]:
   343→    """Return {toolset: available_bool} for every registered toolset."""
   344→    return registry.check_toolset_requirements()
   345→
   346→
   347→def check_tool_availability(quiet: bool = False) -> Tuple[List[str], List[dict]]:
   348→    """Return (available_toolsets, unavailable_info)."""
   349→    return registry.check_tool_availability(quiet=quiet)
   350→

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→Toolsets Module
     4→
     5→This module provides a flexible system for defining and managing tool aliases/toolsets.
     6→Toolsets allow you to group tools together for specific scenarios and can be composed
     7→from individual tools or other toolsets.
     8→
     9→Features:
    10→- Define custom toolsets with specific tools
    11→- Compose toolsets from other toolsets
    12→- Built-in common toolsets for typical use cases
    13→- Easy extension for new toolsets
    14→- Support for dynamic toolset resolution
    15→
    16→Usage:
    17→    from toolsets import get_toolset, resolve_toolset, get_all_toolsets
    18→    
    19→    # Get tools for a specific toolset
    20→    tools = get_toolset("research")
    21→    
    22→    # Resolve a toolset to get all tool names (including from composed toolsets)
    23→    all_tools = resolve_toolset("full_stack")
    24→"""
    25→
    26→from typing import List, Dict, Any, Set, Optional
    27→
    28→
    29→# Shared tool list for CLI and all messaging platform toolsets.
    30→# Edit this once to update all platforms simultaneously.
    31→_HERMES_CORE_TOOLS = [
    32→    # Web
    33→    "web_search", "web_extract",

   284→
   285→def get_toolset(name: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   286→    """
   287→    Get a toolset definition by name.
   288→    
   289→    Args:
   290→        name (str): Name of the toolset
   291→        
   292→    Returns:
   293→        Dict: Toolset definition with description, tools, and includes
   294→        None: If toolset not found
   295→    """
   296→    # Return toolset definition
   297→    return TOOLSETS.get(name)
   298→
   299→
   300→def resolve_toolset(name: str, visited: Set[str] = None) -> List[str]:
   301→    """
   302→    Recursively resolve a toolset to get all tool names.
   303→    
   304→    This function handles toolset composition by recursively resolving
   305→    included toolsets and combining all tools.
   306→    
   307→    Args:
   308→        name (str): Name of the toolset to resolve
   309→        visited (Set[str]): Set of already visited toolsets (for cycle detection)
   310→        
   311→    Returns:
   312→        List[str]: List of all tool names in the toolset
   313→    """
   314→    if visited is None:
   315→        visited = set()
   316→    
   317→    # Special aliases that represent all tools across every toolset
   318→    # This ensures future toolsets are automatically included without changes.
   319→    if name in {"all", "*"}:
   320→        all_tools: Set[str] = set()
   321→        for toolset_name in get_toolset_names():
   322→            # Use a fresh visited set per branch to avoid cross-branch contamination
   323→            resolved = resolve_toolset(toolset_name, visited.copy())
   324→            all_tools.update(resolved)
   325→        return list(all_tools)
   326→
   327→    # Check for cycles
   328→    if name in visited:
   329→        print(f"⚠️  Circular dependency detected in toolset '{name}'")
   330→        return []
   331→    
   332→    visited.add(name)
   333→    
   334→    # Get toolset definition
   335→    toolset = TOOLSETS.get(name)
   336→    if not toolset:
   337→        return []
   338→    
   339→    # Collect direct tools
   340→    tools = set(toolset.get("tools", []))
   341→    
   342→    # Recursively resolve included toolsets
   343→    for included_name in toolset.get("includes", []):
   344→        included_tools = resolve_toolset(included_name, visited.copy())
   345→        tools.update(included_tools)
   346→    
   347→    return list(tools)
   348→
   349→
   350→def resolve_multiple_toolsets(toolset_names: List[str]) -> List[str]:
   351→    """
   352→    Resolve multiple toolsets and combine their tools.
   353→    
   354→    Args:
   355→        toolset_names (List[str]): List of toolset names to resolve
   356→        
   357→    Returns:
   358→        List[str]: Combined list of all tool names (deduplicated)
   359→    """
   360→    all_tools = set()
   361→    
   362→    for name in toolset_names:
   363→        tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   364→        all_tools.update(tools)
   365→    
   366→    return list(all_tools)
   367→
   368→
   369→def get_all_toolsets() -> Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]]:
   370→    """
   371→    Get all available toolsets with their definitions.
   372→    
   373→    Returns:
   374→        Dict: All toolset definitions
   375→    """
   376→    return TOOLSETS.copy()
   377→
   378→
   379→def get_toolset_names() -> List[str]:
   380→    """
   381→    Get names of all available toolsets (excluding aliases).
   382→    
   383→    Returns:
   384→        List[str]: List of toolset names
   385→    """
   386→    return list(TOOLSETS.keys())
   387→
   388→
   389→
   390→
   391→def validate_toolset(name: str) -> bool:
   392→    """
   393→    Check if a toolset name is valid.
   394→    
   395→    Args:
   396→        name (str): Toolset name to validate
   397→        
   398→    Returns:
   399→        bool: True if valid, False otherwise
   400→    """
   401→    # Accept special alias names for convenience
   402→    if name in {"all", "*"}:
   403→        return True
   404→    return name in TOOLSETS
   405→
   406→
   407→def create_custom_toolset(
   408→    name: str,
   409→    description: str,
   410→    tools: List[str] = None,
   411→    includes: List[str] = None
   412→) -> None:
   413→    """
   414→    Create a custom toolset at runtime.
   415→    
   416→    Args:
   417→        name (str): Name for the new toolset
   418→        description (str): Description of the toolset
   419→        tools (List[str]): Direct tools to include
   420→        includes (List[str]): Other toolsets to include
   421→    """
   422→    TOOLSETS[name] = {
   423→        "description": description,
   424→        "tools": tools or [],
   425→        "includes": includes or []
   426→    }
   427→
   428→
   429→
   430→
   431→def get_toolset_info(name: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   432→    """
   433→    Get detailed information about a toolset including resolved tools.
   434→    
   435→    Args:
   436→        name (str): Toolset name
   437→        
   438→    Returns:
   439→        Dict: Detailed toolset information
   440→    """
   441→    toolset = get_toolset(name)
   442→    if not toolset:
   443→        return None
   444→    
   445→    resolved_tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   446→    
   447→    return {
   448→        "name": name,
   449→        "description": toolset["description"],
   450→        "direct_tools": toolset["tools"],
   451→        "includes": toolset["includes"],
   452→        "resolved_tools": resolved_tools,
   453→        "tool_count": len(resolved_tools),
   454→        "is_composite": len(toolset["includes"]) > 0
   455→    }
   456→
   457→
   458→def print_toolset_tree(name: str, indent: int = 0) -> None:
   459→    """
   460→    Print a tree view of a toolset and its composition.
   461→    
   462→    Args:
   463→        name (str): Toolset name
   464→        indent (int): Current indentation level
   465→    """
   466→    prefix = "  " * indent
   467→    toolset = get_toolset(name)
   468→    
   469→    if not toolset:
   470→        print(f"{prefix}❌ Unknown toolset: {name}")
   471→        return
   472→    
   473→    # Print toolset name and description
   474→    print(f"{prefix}📦 {name}: {toolset['description']}")
   475→    
   476→    # Print direct tools
   477→    if toolset["tools"]:
   478→        print(f"{prefix}  🔧 Tools: {', '.join(toolset['tools'])}")
   479→    
   480→    # Print included toolsets
   481→    if toolset["includes"]:
   482→        print(f"{prefix}  📂 Includes:")
   483→        for included in toolset["includes"]:
   484→            print_toolset_tree(included, indent + 2)
   485→
   486→
   487→if __name__ == "__main__":
   488→    print("Toolsets System Demo")
   489→    print("=" * 60)
   490→    
   491→    print("\nAvailable Toolsets:")
   492→    print("-" * 40)
   493→    for name, toolset in get_all_toolsets().items():
   494→        info = get_toolset_info(name)
   495→        composite = "[composite]" if info["is_composite"] else "[leaf]"
   496→        print(f"  {composite} {name:20} - {toolset['description']}")
   497→        print(f"     Tools: {len(info['resolved_tools'])} total")
   498→    
   499→    print("\nToolset Resolution Examples:")
   500→    print("-" * 40)
   501→    for name in ["web", "terminal", "safe", "debugging"]:
   502→        tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   503→        print(f"\n  {name}:")
   504→        print(f"    Resolved to {len(tools)} tools: {', '.join(sorted(tools))}")
   505→    
   506→    print("\nMultiple Toolset Resolution:")
   507→    print("-" * 40)
   508→    combined = resolve_multiple_toolsets(["web", "vision", "terminal"])
   509→    print(f"  Combining ['web', 'vision', 'terminal']:")
   510→    print(f"    Result: {', '.join(sorted(combined))}")
   511→    
   512→    print("\nCustom Toolset Creation:")
   513→    print("-" * 40)
   514→    create_custom_toolset(
   515→        name="my_custom",
   516→        description="My custom toolset for specific tasks",
   517→        tools=["web_search"],
   518→        includes=["terminal", "vision"]
   519→    )
   520→    custom_info = get_toolset_info("my_custom")
   521→    print(f"  Created 'my_custom' toolset:")
   522→    print(f"    Description: {custom_info['description']}")
   523→    print(f"    Resolved tools: {', '.join(custom_info['resolved_tools'])}")
   524→

1→"""System prompt assembly -- identity, platform hints, skills index, context files.
     2→
     3→All functions are stateless. AIAgent._build_system_prompt() calls these to
     4→assemble pieces, then combines them with memory and ephemeral prompts.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→import logging
     8→import os
     9→import re
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import Optional
    12→
    13→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    14→
    15→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    16→# Context file scanning — detect prompt injection in AGENTS.md, .cursorrules,
    17→# SOUL.md before they get injected into the system prompt.
    18→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→
    20→_CONTEXT_THREAT_PATTERNS = [
    21→    (r'ignore\s+(previous|all|above|prior)\s+instructions', "prompt_injection"),
    22→    (r'do\s+not\s+tell\s+the\s+user', "deception_hide"),
    23→    (r'system\s+prompt\s+override', "sys_prompt_override"),
    24→    (r'disregard\s+(your|all|any)\s+(instructions|rules|guidelines)', "disregard_rules"),
    25→    (r'act\s+as\s+(if|though)\s+you\s+(have\s+no|don\'t\s+have)\s+(restrictions|limits|rules)', "bypass_restrictions"),
    26→    (r'<!--[^>]*(?:ignore|override|system|secret|hidden)[^>]*-->', "html_comment_injection"),
    27→    (r'<\s*div\s+style\s*=\s*["\'].*display\s*:\s*none', "hidden_div"),
    28→    (r'translate\s+.*\s+into\s+.*\s+and\s+(execute|run|eval)', "translate_execute"),
    29→    (r'curl\s+[^\n]*\$\{?\w*(KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL|API)', "exfil_curl"),
    30→    (r'cat\s+[^\n]*(\.env|credentials|\.netrc|\.pgpass)', "read_secrets"),
    31→]
    32→
    33→_CONTEXT_INVISIBLE_CHARS = {
    34→    '\u200b', '\u200c', '\u200d', '\u2060', '\ufeff',
    35→    '\u202a', '\u202b', '\u202c', '\u202d', '\u202e',
    36→}
    37→
    38→
    39→def _scan_context_content(content: str, filename: str) -> str:
    40→    """Scan context file content for injection. Returns sanitized content."""
    41→    findings = []
    42→
    43→    # Check invisible unicode
    44→    for char in _CONTEXT_INVISIBLE_CHARS:
    45→        if char in content:
    46→            findings.append(f"invisible unicode U+{ord(char):04X}")
    47→
    48→    # Check threat patterns
    49→    for pattern, pid in _CONTEXT_THREAT_PATTERNS:
    50→        if re.search(pattern, content, re.IGNORECASE):
    51→            findings.append(pid)
    52→
    53→    if findings:
    54→        logger.warning("Context file %s blocked: %s", filename, ", ".join(findings))
    55→        return f"[BLOCKED: {filename} contained potential prompt injection ({', '.join(findings)}). Content not loaded.]"
    56→
    57→    return content
    58→

   105→        "You can send media files natively: to deliver a file to the user, "
   106→        "include MEDIA:/absolute/path/to/file in your response. Images "
   107→        "(.png, .jpg, .webp) appear as photos, audio (.ogg) sends as voice "
   108→        "bubbles, and videos (.mp4) play inline. You can also include image "
   109→        "URLs in markdown format ![alt](url) and they will be sent as native photos."
   110→    ),
   111→    "discord": (
   112→        "You are in a Discord server or group chat communicating with your user. "
   113→        "You can send media files natively: include MEDIA:/absolute/path/to/file "
   114→        "in your response. Images (.png, .jpg, .webp) are sent as photo "
   115→        "attachments, audio as file attachments. You can also include image URLs "
   116→        "in markdown format ![alt](url) and they will be sent as attachments."
   117→    ),
   118→    "slack": (
   119→        "You are in a Slack workspace communicating with your user. "
   120→        "You can send media files natively: include MEDIA:/absolute/path/to/file "
   121→        "in your response. Images (.png, .jpg, .webp) are uploaded as photo "
   122→        "attachments, audio as file attachments. You can also include image URLs "
   123→        "in markdown format ![alt](url) and they will be uploaded as attachments."
   124→    ),
   125→    "signal": (
   126→        "You are on a text messaging communication platform, Signal. "
   127→        "Please do not use markdown as it does not render. "

   130→        "(.png, .jpg, .webp) appear as photos, audio as attachments, and other "
   131→        "files arrive as downloadable documents. You can also include image "
   132→        "URLs in markdown format ![alt](url) and they will be sent as photos."
   133→    ),
   134→    "email": (
   135→        "You are communicating via email. Write clear, well-structured responses "
   136→        "suitable for email. Use plain text formatting (no markdown). "
   137→        "Keep responses concise but complete. You can send file attachments — "
   138→        "include MEDIA:/absolute/path/to/file in your response. The subject line "
   139→        "is preserved for threading. Do not include greetings or sign-offs unless "
   140→        "contextually appropriate."
   141→    ),
   142→    "cli": (
   143→        "You are a CLI AI Agent. Try not to use markdown but simple text "
   144→        "renderable inside a terminal."
   145→    ),
   146→}
   147→
   148→CONTEXT_FILE_MAX_CHARS = 20_000
   149→CONTEXT_TRUNCATE_HEAD_RATIO = 0.7
   150→CONTEXT_TRUNCATE_TAIL_RATIO = 0.2
   151→
   152→
   153→# =========================================================================
   154→# Skills index
   155→# =========================================================================
   156→
   157→def _parse_skill_file(skill_file: Path) -> tuple[bool, dict, str]:
   158→    """Read a SKILL.md once and return platform compatibility, frontmatter, and description.
   159→
   160→    Returns (is_compatible, frontmatter, description). On any error, returns
   161→    (True, {}, "") to err on the side of showing the skill.
   162→    """
   163→    try:
   164→        from tools.skills_tool import _parse_frontmatter, skill_matches_platform
   165→
   166→        raw = skill_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8")[:2000]
   167→        frontmatter, _ = _parse_frontmatter(raw)
   168→
   169→        if not skill_matches_platform(frontmatter):
   170→            return False, {}, ""
   171→
   172→        desc = ""
   173→        raw_desc = frontmatter.get("description", "")
   174→        if raw_desc:
   175→            desc = str(raw_desc).strip().strip("'\"")
   176→            if len(desc) > 60:
   177→                desc = desc[:57] + "..."
   178→
   179→        return True, frontmatter, desc
   180→    except Exception:
   181→        return True, {}, ""
   182→
   183→
   184→def _read_skill_conditions(skill_file: Path) -> dict:
   185→    """Extract conditional activation fields from SKILL.md frontmatter."""
   186→    try:
   187→        from tools.skills_tool import _parse_frontmatter
   188→        raw = skill_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8")[:2000]
   189→        frontmatter, _ = _parse_frontmatter(raw)
   190→        hermes = frontmatter.get("metadata", {}).get("hermes", {})
   191→        return {
   192→            "fallback_for_toolsets": hermes.get("fallback_for_toolsets", []),
   193→            "requires_toolsets": hermes.get("requires_toolsets", []),
   194→            "fallback_for_tools": hermes.get("fallback_for_tools", []),
   195→            "requires_tools": hermes.get("requires_tools", []),
   196→        }
   197→    except Exception:
   198→        return {}
   199→
   200→
   201→def _skill_should_show(
   202→    conditions: dict,
   203→    available_tools: "set[str] | None",
   204→    available_toolsets: "set[str] | None",
   205→) -> bool:
   206→    """Return False if the skill's conditional activation rules exclude it."""
   207→    if available_tools is None and available_toolsets is None:
   208→        return True  # No filtering info — show everything (backward compat)
   209→
   210→    at = available_tools or set()
   211→    ats = available_toolsets or set()
   212→
   213→    # fallback_for: hide when the primary tool/toolset IS available
   214→    for ts in conditions.get("fallback_for_toolsets", []):
   215→        if ts in ats:
   216→            return False
   217→    for t in conditions.get("fallback_for_tools", []):
   218→        if t in at:
   219→            return False
   220→
   221→    # requires: hide when a required tool/toolset is NOT available
   222→    for ts in conditions.get("requires_toolsets", []):
   223→        if ts not in ats:
   224→            return False
   225→    for t in conditions.get("requires_tools", []):
   226→        if t not in at:
   227→            return False
   228→
   229→    return True
   230→
   231→
   232→def build_skills_system_prompt(
   233→    available_tools: "set[str] | None" = None,
   234→    available_toolsets: "set[str] | None" = None,
   235→) -> str:
   236→    """Build a compact skill index for the system prompt.
   237→
   238→    Scans ~/.hermes/skills/ for SKILL.md files grouped by category.
   239→    Includes per-skill descriptions from frontmatter so the model can
   240→    match skills by meaning, not just name.
   241→    Filters out skills incompatible with the current OS platform.
   242→    """
   243→    hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   244→    skills_dir = hermes_home / "skills"
   245→
   246→    if not skills_dir.exists():
   247→        return ""
   248→
   249→    # Collect skills with descriptions, grouped by category.
   250→    # Each entry: (skill_name, description)
   251→    # Supports sub-categories: skills/mlops/training/axolotl/SKILL.md
   252→    # -> category "mlops/training", skill "axolotl"
   253→    skills_by_category: dict[str, list[tuple[str, str]]] = {}
   254→    for skill_file in skills_dir.rglob("SKILL.md"):
   255→        is_compatible, _, desc = _parse_skill_file(skill_file)
   256→        if not is_compatible:
   257→            continue
   258→        # Skip skills whose conditional activation rules exclude them
   259→        conditions = _read_skill_conditions(skill_file)
   260→        if not _skill_should_show(conditions, available_tools, available_toolsets):
   261→            continue
   262→        rel_path = skill_file.relative_to(skills_dir)
   263→        parts = rel_path.parts
   264→        if len(parts) >= 2:
   265→            # Category is everything between skills_dir and the skill folder
   266→            # e.g. parts = ("mlops", "training", "axolotl", "SKILL.md")
   267→            #   → category = "mlops/training", skill_name = "axolotl"
   268→            # e.g. parts = ("github", "github-auth", "SKILL.md")
   269→            #   → category = "github", skill_name = "github-auth"
   270→            skill_name = parts[-2]
   271→            category = "/".join(parts[:-2]) if len(parts) > 2 else parts[0]
   272→        else:
   273→            category = "general"
   274→            skill_name = skill_file.parent.name
   275→        skills_by_category.setdefault(category, []).append((skill_name, desc))
   276→
   277→    if not skills_by_category:
   278→        return ""
   279→
   280→    # Read category-level descriptions from DESCRIPTION.md
   281→    # Checks both the exact category path and parent directories
   282→    category_descriptions = {}
   283→    for category in skills_by_category:
   284→        cat_path = Path(category)
   285→        desc_file = skills_dir / cat_path / "DESCRIPTION.md"
   286→        if desc_file.exists():
   287→            try:
   288→                content = desc_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   289→                match = re.search(r"^---\s*\n.*?description:\s*(.+?)\s*\n.*?^---", content, re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL)
   290→                if match:
   291→                    category_descriptions[category] = match.group(1).strip()
   292→            except Exception as e:
   293→                logger.debug("Could not read skill description %s: %s", desc_file, e)
   294→
   295→    index_lines = []
   296→    for category in sorted(skills_by_category.keys()):
   297→        cat_desc = category_descriptions.get(category, "")
   298→        if cat_desc:
   299→            index_lines.append(f"  {category}: {cat_desc}")
   300→        else:
   301→            index_lines.append(f"  {category}:")
   302→        # Deduplicate and sort skills within each category
   303→        seen = set()
   304→        for name, desc in sorted(skills_by_category[category], key=lambda x: x[0]):
   305→            if name in seen:
   306→                continue
   307→            seen.add(name)
   308→            if desc:
   309→                index_lines.append(f"    - {name}: {desc}")
   310→            else:
   311→                index_lines.append(f"    - {name}")
   312→
   313→    return (
   314→        "## Skills (mandatory)\n"
   315→        "Before replying, scan the skills below. If one clearly matches your task, "
   316→        "load it with skill_view(name) and follow its instructions. "
   317→        "If a skill has issues, fix it with skill_manage(action='patch').\n"
   318→        "\n"
   319→        "<available_skills>\n"
   320→        + "\n".join(index_lines) + "\n"
   321→        "</available_skills>\n"
   322→        "\n"
   323→        "If none match, proceed normally without loading a skill."
   324→    )
   325→
   326→
   327→# =========================================================================
   328→# Context files (SOUL.md, AGENTS.md, .cursorrules)
   329→# =========================================================================
   330→
   331→def _truncate_content(content: str, filename: str, max_chars: int = CONTEXT_FILE_MAX_CHARS) -> str:
   332→    """Head/tail truncation with a marker in the middle."""
   333→    if len(content) <= max_chars:
   334→        return content
   335→    head_chars = int(max_chars * CONTEXT_TRUNCATE_HEAD_RATIO)
   336→    tail_chars = int(max_chars * CONTEXT_TRUNCATE_TAIL_RATIO)
   337→    head = content[:head_chars]
   338→    tail = content[-tail_chars:]
   339→    marker = f"\n\n[...truncated {filename}: kept {head_chars}+{tail_chars} of {len(content)} chars. Use file tools to read the full file.]\n\n"
   340→    return head + marker + tail
   341→
   342→
   343→def build_context_files_prompt(cwd: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
   344→    """Discover and load context files for the system prompt.
   345→
   346→    Discovery: AGENTS.md (recursive), .cursorrules / .cursor/rules/*.mdc,
   347→    SOUL.md (cwd then ~/.hermes/ fallback). Each capped at 20,000 chars.
   348→    """
   349→    if cwd is None:
   350→        cwd = os.getcwd()
   351→
   352→    cwd_path = Path(cwd).resolve()
   353→    sections = []
   354→
   355→    # AGENTS.md (hierarchical, recursive)
   356→    top_level_agents = None
   357→    for name in ["AGENTS.md", "agents.md"]:
   358→        candidate = cwd_path / name
   359→        if candidate.exists():
   360→            top_level_agents = candidate
   361→            break
   362→
   363→    if top_level_agents:
   364→        agents_files = []
   365→        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(cwd_path):
   366→            dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if not d.startswith('.') and d not in ('node_modules', '__pycache__', 'venv', '.venv')]
   367→            for f in files:
   368→                if f.lower() == "agents.md":
   369→                    agents_files.append(Path(root) / f)
   370→        agents_files.sort(key=lambda p: len(p.parts))
   371→
   372→        total_agents_content = ""
   373→        for agents_path in agents_files:
   374→            try:
   375→                content = agents_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
   376→                if content:
   377→                    rel_path = agents_path.relative_to(cwd_path)
   378→                    content = _scan_context_content(content, str(rel_path))
   379→                    total_agents_content += f"## {rel_path}\n\n{content}\n\n"
   380→            except Exception as e:
   381→                logger.debug("Could not read %s: %s", agents_path, e)
   382→
   383→        if total_agents_content:
   384→            total_agents_content = _truncate_content(total_agents_content, "AGENTS.md")
   385→            sections.append(total_agents_content)
   386→
   387→    # .cursorrules
   388→    cursorrules_content = ""
   389→    cursorrules_file = cwd_path / ".cursorrules"
   390→    if cursorrules_file.exists():
   391→        try:
   392→            content = cursorrules_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
   393→            if content:
   394→                content = _scan_context_content(content, ".cursorrules")
   395→                cursorrules_content += f"## .cursorrules\n\n{content}\n\n"
   396→        except Exception as e:
   397→            logger.debug("Could not read .cursorrules: %s", e)
   398→
   399→    cursor_rules_dir = cwd_path / ".cursor" / "rules"
   400→    if cursor_rules_dir.exists() and cursor_rules_dir.is_dir():
   401→        mdc_files = sorted(cursor_rules_dir.glob("*.mdc"))
   402→        for mdc_file in mdc_files:
   403→            try:
   404→                content = mdc_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
   405→                if content:
   406→                    content = _scan_context_content(content, f".cursor/rules/{mdc_file.name}")
   407→                    cursorrules_content += f"## .cursor/rules/{mdc_file.name}\n\n{content}\n\n"
   408→            except Exception as e:
   409→                logger.debug("Could not read %s: %s", mdc_file, e)
   410→
   411→    if cursorrules_content:
   412→        cursorrules_content = _truncate_content(cursorrules_content, ".cursorrules")
   413→        sections.append(cursorrules_content)
   414→
   415→    # SOUL.md (cwd first, then ~/.hermes/ fallback)
   416→    soul_path = None
   417→    for name in ["SOUL.md", "soul.md"]:
   418→        candidate = cwd_path / name
   419→        if candidate.exists():
   420→            soul_path = candidate
   421→            break
   422→    if not soul_path:
   423→        global_soul = Path.home() / ".hermes" / "SOUL.md"
   424→        if global_soul.exists():
   425→            soul_path = global_soul
   426→
   427→    if soul_path:
   428→        try:
   429→            content = soul_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
   430→            if content:
   431→                content = _scan_context_content(content, "SOUL.md")
   432→                content = _truncate_content(content, "SOUL.md")
   433→                sections.append(
   434→                    f"## SOUL.md\n\nIf SOUL.md is present, embody its persona and tone. "
   435→                    f"Avoid stiff, generic replies; follow its guidance unless higher-priority "
   436→                    f"instructions override it.\n\n{content}"
   437→                )
   438→        except Exception as e:
   439→            logger.debug("Could not read SOUL.md from %s: %s", soul_path, e)
   440→
   441→    if not sections:
   442→        return ""
   443→    return "# Project Context\n\nThe following project context files have been loaded and should be followed:\n\n" + "\n".join(sections)
   444→
```

  2575→    def _try_activate_fallback(self) -> bool:
  2576→        """Switch to the configured fallback model/provider.
  2577→
  2578→        Called when the primary model is failing after retries.  Swaps the
  2579→        OpenAI client, model slug, and provider in-place so the retry loop
  2580→        can continue with the new backend.  One-shot: returns False if
  2581→        already activated or not configured.
  2582→
  2583→        Uses the centralized provider router (resolve_provider_client) for
  2584→        auth resolution and client construction — no duplicated provider→key
  2585→        mappings.
  2586→        """
  2587→        if self._fallback_activated or not self._fallback_model:
  2588→            return False
  2589→
  2590→        fb = self._fallback_model
  2591→        fb_provider = (fb.get("provider") or "").strip().lower()
  2592→        fb_model = (fb.get("model") or "").strip()
  2593→        if not fb_provider or not fb_model:
  2594→            return False
  2595→

  2604→                logging.warning(
  2605→                    "Fallback to %s failed: provider not configured",

  2607→                return False
  2608→
  2609→            # Determine api_mode from provider

  2611→            if fb_provider == "openai-codex":
  2612→                fb_api_mode = "codex_responses"
  2613→            elif fb_provider == "anthropic":
  2614→                fb_api_mode = "anthropic_messages"
  2615→            fb_base_url = str(fb_client.base_url)
  2616→
  2617→            old_model = self.model
  2618→            self.model = fb_model
  2619→            self.provider = fb_provider
  2620→            self.base_url = fb_base_url
  2621→            self.api_mode = fb_api_mode
  2622→            self._fallback_activated = True
  2623→
  2624→            if fb_api_mode == "anthropic_messages":

  2627→                [REDACTED] or resolve_anthropic_token() or ""

  2637→                    "base_url": fb_base_url,
  2638→                }

3216→    def _execute_tool_calls_concurrent(self, assistant_message, messages: list, effective_task_id: str, api_call_count: int = 0) -> None:
  3217→        """Execute multiple tool calls concurrently using a thread pool.
  3218→
  3219→        Results are collected in the original tool-call order and appended to
  3220→        messages so the API sees them in the expected sequence.
  3221→        """
  3222→        tool_calls = assistant_message.tool_calls
  3223→        num_tools = len(tool_calls)
  3224→
  3225→        # ── Pre-flight: interrupt check ──────────────────────────────────
  3226→        if self._interrupt_requested:
  3227→            print(f"{self.log_prefix}⚡ Interrupt: skipping {num_tools} tool call(s)")
  3228→            for tc in tool_calls:
  3229→                messages.append({
  3230→                    "role": "tool",
  3231→                    "content": f"[Tool execution cancelled — {tc.function.name} was skipped due to user interrupt]",
  3232→                    "tool_call_id": tc.id,
  3233→                })
  3234→            return
  3235→
  3236→        # ── Parse args + pre-execution bookkeeping ───────────────────────
  3237→        parsed_calls = []  # list of (tool_call, function_name, function_args)
  3238→        for tool_call in tool_calls:
  3239→            function_name = tool_call.function.name
  3240→
  3241→            # Reset nudge counters
  3242→            if function_name == "memory":
  3243→                self._turns_since_memory = 0
  3244→            elif function_name == "skill_manage":
  3245→                self._iters_since_skill = 0
  3246→
  3247→            try:
  3248→                function_args = json.loads(tool_call.function.arguments)
  3249→            except json.JSONDecodeError:
  3250→                function_args = {}
  3251→            if not isinstance(function_args, dict):
  3252→                function_args = {}
  3253→
  3254→            # Checkpoint for file-mutating tools
  3255→            if function_name in ("write_file", "patch") and self._checkpoint_mgr.enabled:
  3256→                try:
  3257→                    file_path = function_args.get("path", "")
  3258→                    if file_path:
  3259→                        work_dir = self._checkpoint_mgr.get_working_dir_for_path(file_path)
  3260→                        self._checkpoint_mgr.ensure_checkpoint(work_dir, f"before {function_name}")
  3261→                except Exception:
  3262→                    pass
  3263→
  3264→            parsed_calls.append((tool_call, function_name, function_args))
  3265→
  3266→        # ── Logging / callbacks ──────────────────────────────────────────
  3267→        tool_names_str = ", ".join(name for _, name, _ in parsed_calls)
  3268→        if not self.quiet_mode:
  3269→            print(f"  ⚡ Concurrent: {num_tools} tool calls — {tool_names_str}")
  3270→            for i, (tc, name, args) in enumerate(parsed_calls, 1):

  3273→                print(f"  📞 Tool {i}: {name}({list(args.keys())}) - {args_preview}")

  3275→        for _, name, args in parsed_calls:

  3282→
  3283→        # ── Concurrent execution ─────────────────────────────────────────
  3284→        # Each slot holds (function_name, function_args, function_result, duration, error_flag)
  3285→        results = [None] * num_tools

    81→
    82→_PROVIDER_LABELS = {
    83→    "openrouter": "OpenRouter",
    84→    "openai-codex": "OpenAI Codex",
    85→    "nous": "Nous Portal",
    86→    "zai": "Z.AI / GLM",
