    name: str,                           # Tool name
    toolset: str,                        # Group it belongs to
    schema: dict,                        # OpenAI function schema
    handler: Callable,                   # Function to call
    check_fn: Callable = None,           # Availability check
    requires_env: list = None,           # Required env vars (documentation)
    is_async: bool = False,              # Is the handler async?
    description: str = "",               # Override schema description
):
    """Register a tool. Called at module-import time by each tool file."""
    self._tools[name] = ToolEntry(...)
```

**Dispatch Flow (lines 112-129):**
```python
def dispatch(self, name: str, args: dict, **kwargs) -> str:
    """Execute a tool handler by name.
    
    * Async handlers are bridged automatically via ``_run_async()``.
    * All exceptions are caught and returned as ``{"error": "..."}``
    """
    entry = self._tools.get(name)
    if not entry:
        return json.dumps({"error": f"Unknown tool: {name}"})
    try:
        if entry.is_async:
            from model_tools import _run_async
            return _run_async(entry.handler(args, **kwargs))
        return entry.handler(args, **kwargs)
    except Exception as e:
        logger.exception("Tool %s dispatch error: %s", name, e)
        return json.dumps({"error": f"Tool execution failed: {type(e).__name__}: {e}"})
```

This architecture ensures:
- ✅ Clean separation of concerns
- ✅ Circular import safety
- ✅ Runtime discoverability
- ✅ Easy enable/disable via toolsets

# Model configuration

> Learn about the Claude Code model configuration, including model aliases like `opusplan`

## Available models

For the `model` setting in Claude Code, you can configure either:

* A **model alias**
* A **model name**
  * Anthropic API: A full **[model name](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/overview)**
  * Bedrock: an inference profile ARN
  * Foundry: a deployment name
  * Vertex: a version name

### Model aliases

Model aliases provide a convenient way to select model settings without
remembering exact version numbers:

| Model alias      | Behavior                                                                                                                                                             |
| ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`default`**    | Recommended model setting, depending on your account type                                                                                                            |
| **`sonnet`**     | Uses the latest Sonnet model (currently Sonnet 4.6) for daily coding tasks                                                                                           |
| **`opus`**       | Uses the latest Opus model (currently Opus 4.6) for complex reasoning tasks                                                                                          |
| **`haiku`**      | Uses the fast and efficient Haiku model for simple tasks                                                                                                             |
| **`sonnet[1m]`** | Uses Sonnet with a [1 million token context window](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/context-windows#1m-token-context-window) for long sessions |
| **`opus[1m]`**   | Uses Opus with a [1 million token context window](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/context-windows#1m-token-context-window) for long sessions   |
| **`opusplan`**   | Special mode that uses `opus` during plan mode, then switches to `sonnet` for execution                                                                              |

Aliases always point to the latest version. To pin to a specific version, use the full model name (for example, `claude-opus-4-6`) or set the corresponding environment variable like `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL`.

### Setting your model

You can configure your model in several ways, listed in order of priority:

1. **During session** - Use `/model <alias|name>` to switch models mid-session
2. **At startup** - Launch with `claude --model <alias|name>`
3. **Environment variable** - Set `ANTHROPIC_MODEL=<alias|name>`
4. **Settings** - Configure permanently in your settings file using the `model`
   field.

Example usage:

```bash  theme={null}
# Start with Opus
claude --model opus

# Switch to Sonnet during session
/model sonnet
```

Example settings file:

```json  theme={null}
{
    "permissions": {
        ...
    },
    "model": "opus"
}
```

## Restrict model selection

Enterprise administrators can use `availableModels` in [managed or policy settings](/en/settings#settings-files) to restrict which models users can select.

When `availableModels` is set, users cannot switch to models not in the list via `/model`, `--model` flag, Config tool, or `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` environment variable.

```json  theme={null}
{
  "availableModels": ["sonnet", "haiku"]
}
```

### Default model behavior

The Default option in the model picker is not affected by `availableModels`. It always remains available and represents the system's runtime default [based on the user's subscription tier](#default-model-setting).

Even with `availableModels: []`, users can still use Claude Code with the Default model for their tier.

### Control the model users run on

To fully control the model experience, use `availableModels` together with the `model` setting:

* **availableModels**: restricts what users can switch to
* **model**: sets the explicit model override, taking precedence over the Default

This example ensures all users run Sonnet 4.6 and can only choose between Sonnet and Haiku:

```json  theme={null}
{
  "model": "sonnet",
  "availableModels": ["sonnet", "haiku"]
}
```

### Merge behavior

When `availableModels` is set at multiple levels, such as user settings and project settings, arrays are merged and deduplicated. To enforce a strict allowlist, set `availableModels` in managed or policy settings which take highest priority.

## Special model behavior

### `default` model setting

The behavior of `default` depends on your account type:

* **Max and Team Premium**: defaults to Opus 4.6
* **Pro and Team Standard**: defaults to Sonnet 4.6
* **Enterprise**: Opus 4.6 is available but not the default

Claude Code may automatically fall back to Sonnet if you hit a usage threshold with Opus.

### `opusplan` model setting

The `opusplan` model alias provides an automated hybrid approach:

* **In plan mode** - Uses `opus` for complex reasoning and architecture
  decisions
* **In execution mode** - Automatically switches to `sonnet` for code generation
  and implementation

This gives you the best of both worlds: Opus's superior reasoning for planning,
and Sonnet's efficiency for execution.

### Adjust effort level

[Effort levels](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/effort) control adaptive reasoning, which dynamically allocates thinking based on task complexity. Lower effort is faster and cheaper for straightforward tasks, while higher effort provides deeper reasoning for complex problems.

Three levels are available: **low**, **medium**, and **high**. Opus 4.6 defaults to medium effort for Max and Team subscribers.

**Setting effort:**

* **In `/model`**: use left/right arrow keys to adjust the effort slider when selecting a model
* **Environment variable**: set `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL=low|medium|high`
* **Settings**: set `effortLevel` in your settings file

Effort is supported on Opus 4.6 and Sonnet 4.6. The effort slider appears in `/model` when a supported model is selected. The current effort level is also displayed next to the logo and spinner (for example, "with low effort"), so you can confirm which setting is active without opening `/model`.

To disable adaptive reasoning on Opus 4.6 and Sonnet 4.6 and revert to the previous fixed thinking budget, set `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADAPTIVE_THINKING=1`. When disabled, these models use the fixed budget controlled by `MAX_THINKING_TOKENS`. See [environment variables](/en/env-vars).

### Extended context

Opus 4.6 and Sonnet 4.6 support a [1 million token context window](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/context-windows#1m-token-context-window) for long sessions with large codebases.

Availability varies by model and plan. On Max, Team, and Enterprise plans, Opus is automatically upgraded to 1M context with no additional configuration. This applies to both Team Standard and Team Premium seats.

| Plan                      | Opus 4.6 with 1M context                                                                                  | Sonnet 4.6 with 1M context                                                                                |
| ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Max, Team, and Enterprise | Included with subscription                                                                                | Requires [extra usage](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12429409-extra-usage-for-paid-claude-plans) |
| Pro                       | Requires [extra usage](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12429409-extra-usage-for-paid-claude-plans) | Requires [extra usage](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12429409-extra-usage-for-paid-claude-plans) |
| API and pay-as-you-go     | Full access                                                                                               | Full access                                                                                               |

To disable 1M context entirely, set `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_1M_CONTEXT=1`. This removes 1M model variants from the model picker. See [environment variables](/en/env-vars).

The 1M context window uses standard model pricing with no premium for tokens beyond 200K. For plans where extended context is included with your subscription, usage remains covered by your subscription. For plans that access extended context through extra usage, tokens are billed to extra usage.

If your account supports 1M context, the option appears in the model picker (`/model`) in the latest versions of Claude Code. If you don't see it, try restarting your session.

You can also use the `[1m]` suffix with model aliases or full model names:

```bash  theme={null}
# Use the opus[1m] or sonnet[1m] alias
/model opus[1m]
/model sonnet[1m]

# Or append [1m] to a full model name
/model claude-opus-4-6[1m]
```

## Checking your current model

You can see which model you're currently using in several ways:

1. In [status line](/en/statusline) (if configured)
2. In `/status`, which also displays your account information.

## Environment variables

You can use the following environment variables, which must be full **model
names** (or equivalent for your API provider), to control the model names that the aliases map to.

| Environment variable             | Description                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL`   | The model to use for `opus`, or for `opusplan` when Plan Mode is active.                      |
| `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_SONNET_MODEL` | The model to use for `sonnet`, or for `opusplan` when Plan Mode is not active.                |
| `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`  | The model to use for `haiku`, or [background functionality](/en/costs#background-token-usage) |
| `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`     | The model to use for [subagents](/en/sub-agents)                                              |

Note: `ANTHROPIC_SMALL_FAST_MODEL` is deprecated in favor of
`ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`.

### Pin models for third-party deployments

When deploying Claude Code through [Bedrock](/en/amazon-bedrock), [Vertex AI](/en/google-vertex-ai), or [Foundry](/en/microsoft-foundry), pin model versions before rolling out to users.

Without pinning, Claude Code uses model aliases (`sonnet`, `opus`, `haiku`) that resolve to the latest version. When Anthropic releases a new model, users whose accounts don't have the new version enabled will break silently.

<Warning>
  Set all three model environment variables to specific version IDs as part of your initial setup. Skipping this step means a Claude Code update can break your users without any action on your part.
</Warning>

Use the following environment variables with version-specific model IDs for your provider:

| Provider  | Example                                                                 |
| :-------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Bedrock   | `export ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL='us.anthropic.claude-opus-4-6-v1'` |
| Vertex AI | `export ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL='claude-opus-4-6'`                 |
| Foundry   | `export ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL='claude-opus-4-6'`                 |

Apply the same pattern for `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_SONNET_MODEL` and `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`. For current and legacy model IDs across all providers, see [Models overview](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/overview). To upgrade users to a new model version, update these environment variables and redeploy.

To enable [extended context](#extended-context) for a pinned model, append `[1m]` to the model ID in `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL` or `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_SONNET_MODEL`:

```bash  theme={null}
export ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL='claude-opus-4-6[1m]'
```

The `[1m]` suffix applies the 1M context window to all usage of that alias, including `opusplan`. Claude Code strips the suffix before sending the model ID to your provider. Only append `[1m]` when the underlying model supports 1M context, such as Opus 4.6 or Sonnet 4.6.

<Note>
  The `settings.availableModels` allowlist still applies when using third-party providers. Filtering matches on the model alias (`opus`, `sonnet`, `haiku`), not the provider-specific model ID.
</Note>

### Override model IDs per version

The family-level environment variables above configure one model ID per family alias. If you need to map several versions within the same family to distinct provider IDs, use the `modelOverrides` setting instead.

`modelOverrides` maps individual Anthropic model IDs to the provider-specific strings that Claude Code sends to your provider's API. When a user selects a mapped model in the `/model` picker, Claude Code uses your configured value instead of the built-in default.

This lets enterprise administrators route each model version to a specific Bedrock inference profile ARN, Vertex AI version name, or Foundry deployment name for governance, cost allocation, or regional routing.

Set `modelOverrides` in your [settings file](/en/settings#settings-files):

```json  theme={null}
{
  "modelOverrides": {
    "claude-opus-4-6": "arn:aws:bedrock:us-east-2:123456789012:application-inference-profile/opus-prod",
    "claude-opus-4-5-20251101": "arn:aws:bedrock:us-east-2:123456789012:application-inference-profile/opus-45-prod",
    "claude-sonnet-4-6": "arn:aws:bedrock:us-east-2:123456789012:application-inference-profile/sonnet-prod"
  }
}
```

Keys must be Anthropic model IDs as listed in the [Models overview](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/overview). For dated model IDs, include the date suffix exactly as it appears there. Unknown keys are ignored.

Overrides replace the built-in model IDs that back each entry in the `/model` picker. On Bedrock, overrides take precedence over any inference profiles that Claude Code discovers automatically at startup. Values you supply directly through `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `--model`, or the `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_*_MODEL` environment variables are passed to the provider as-is and are not transformed by `modelOverrides`.

`modelOverrides` works alongside `availableModels`. The allowlist is evaluated against the Anthropic model ID, not the override value, so an entry like `"opus"` in `availableModels` continues to match even when Opus versions are mapped to ARNs.

### Prompt caching configuration

Claude Code automatically uses [prompt caching](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching) to optimize performance and reduce costs. You can disable prompt caching globally or for specific model tiers:

| Environment variable            | Description                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING`        | Set to `1` to disable prompt caching for all models (takes precedence over per-model settings) |
| `DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_HAIKU`  | Set to `1` to disable prompt caching for Haiku models only                                     |
| `DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_SONNET` | Set to `1` to disable prompt caching for Sonnet models only                                    |
| `DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_OPUS`   | Set to `1` to disable prompt caching for Opus models only                                      |

These environment variables give you fine-grained control over prompt caching behavior. The global `DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING` setting takes precedence over the model-specific settings, allowing you to quickly disable all caching when needed. The per-model settings are useful for selective control, such as when debugging specific models or working with cloud providers that may have different caching implementations.
```

Perfect! I found the answer in the official documentation.

Yes, Claude Code has a built-in way to switch models mid-conversation. You can use the **`/model` command** to change between Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, and other models during an active session.

Here's what's available:

**Slash command during session:**
```
/model <alias|name>
```

For example:
```
/model sonnet
/model opus
/model haiku
```

**Other ways to set or switch models:**
1. **At startup** - Use the CLI flag: `claude --model opus`
2. **Environment variable** - Set `ANTHROPIC_MODEL=sonnet`
3. **Settings file** - Configure permanently in your settings file using the `model` field
4. **Interactive picker** - Using `/model` opens an interactive menu where you can:
   - Select from available model aliases (default, sonnet, opus, haiku)
   - Use the **left/right arrow keys** to adjust the **effort level** (low, medium, high) when selecting Opus 4.6 or Sonnet 4.6
   - Optionally choose the **1M context window variant** with `[1m]` suffix (e.g., `opus[1m]`, `sonnet[1m]`)

**Special model aliases available:**
- `default` - Your tier's recommended model
- `sonnet` - Latest Sonnet (currently 4.6)
- `opus` - Latest Opus (currently 4.6)
- `haiku` - Fast and efficient Haiku model
- `opusplan` - Hybrid mode that uses Opus for planning, then auto-switches to Sonnet for execution
- `sonnet[1m]` or `opus[1m]` - Same models with 1 million token context window

When you run `/model`, you get an interactive menu to browse and select your preferred model mid-session. This is useful for switching to a faster model for simple tasks or upgrading to a more capable model when you need it.

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→Delegate Tool -- Subagent Architecture
     4→
     5→Spawns child AIAgent instances with isolated context, restricted toolsets,
     6→and their own terminal sessions. Supports single-task and batch (parallel)
     7→modes. The parent blocks until all children complete.
     8→
     9→Each child gets:
    10→  - A fresh conversation (no parent history)
    11→  - Its own task_id (own terminal session, file ops cache)
    12→  - A restricted toolset (configurable, with blocked tools always stripped)
    13→  - A focused system prompt built from the delegated goal + context
    14→
    15→The parent's context only sees the delegation call and the summary result,
    16→never the child's intermediate tool calls or reasoning.
    17→"""
    18→
    19→import contextlib
    20→import io
    21→import json
    22→import logging
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→import os
    25→import sys
    26→import time
    27→from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
    28→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
    29→
    30→
    31→# Tools that children must never have access to
    32→DELEGATE_BLOCKED_TOOLS = frozenset([
    33→    "delegate_task",   # no recursive delegation
    34→    "clarify",         # no user interaction
    35→    "memory",          # no writes to shared MEMORY.md
    36→    "send_message",    # no cross-platform side effects
    37→    "execute_code",    # children should reason step-by-step, not write scripts
    38→])
    39→
    40→MAX_CONCURRENT_CHILDREN = 3
    41→MAX_DEPTH = 2  # parent (0) -> child (1) -> grandchild rejected (2)
    42→DEFAULT_MAX_ITERATIONS = 50
    43→DEFAULT_TOOLSETS = ["terminal", "file", "web"]
    44→
    45→
    46→def check_delegate_requirements() -> bool:
    47→    """Delegation has no external requirements -- always available."""
    48→    return True
    49→
    50→
    51→def _build_child_system_prompt(goal: str, context: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
    52→    """Build a focused system prompt for a child agent."""
    53→    parts = [
    54→        "You are a focused subagent working on a specific delegated task.",
    55→        "",
    56→        f"YOUR TASK:\n{goal}",
    57→    ]
    58→    if context and context.strip():
    59→        parts.append(f"\nCONTEXT:\n{context}")
    60→    parts.append(
    61→        "\nComplete this task using the tools available to you. "
    62→        "When finished, provide a clear, concise summary of:\n"
    63→        "- What you did\n"
    64→        "- What you found or accomplished\n"
    65→        "- Any files you created or modified\n"
    66→        "- Any issues encountered\n\n"
    67→        "Be thorough but concise -- your response is returned to the "
    68→        "parent agent as a summary."
    69→    )
    70→    return "\n".join(parts)
    71→
    72→
    73→def _strip_blocked_tools(toolsets: List[str]) -> List[str]:
    74→    """Remove toolsets that contain only blocked tools."""
    75→    blocked_toolset_names = {
    76→        "delegation", "clarify", "memory", "code_execution",
    77→    }
    78→    return [t for t in toolsets if t not in blocked_toolset_names]
    79→
    80→
    81→def _build_child_progress_callback(task_index: int, parent_agent, task_count: int = 1) -> Optional[callable]:
    82→    """Build a callback that relays child agent tool calls to the parent display.
    83→
    84→    Two display paths:
    85→      CLI:     prints tree-view lines above the parent's delegation spinner
    86→      Gateway: batches tool names and relays to parent's progress callback
    87→
    88→    Returns None if no display mechanism is available, in which case the
    89→    child agent runs with no progress callback (identical to current behavior).
    90→    """
    91→    spinner = getattr(parent_agent, '_delegate_spinner', None)
    92→    parent_cb = getattr(parent_agent, 'tool_progress_callback', None)
    93→
    94→    if not spinner and not parent_cb:
    95→        return None  # No display → no callback → zero behavior change
    96→
    97→    # Show 1-indexed prefix only in batch mode (multiple tasks)
    98→    prefix = f"[{task_index + 1}] " if task_count > 1 else ""
    99→
   100→    # Gateway: batch tool names, flush periodically
   101→    _BATCH_SIZE = 5
   102→    _batch: List[str] = []
   103→
   104→    def _callback(tool_name: str, preview: str = None):
   105→        # Special "_thinking" event: model produced text content (reasoning)
   106→        if tool_name == "_thinking":
   107→            if spinner:
   108→                short = (preview[:55] + "...") if preview and len(preview) > 55 else (preview or "")
   109→                try:
   110→                    spinner.print_above(f" {prefix}├─ 💭 \"{short}\"")
   111→                except Exception as e:
   112→                    logger.debug("Spinner print_above failed: %s", e)
   113→            # Don't relay thinking to gateway (too noisy for chat)
   114→            return
   115→
   116→        # Regular tool call event
   117→        if spinner:
   118→            short = (preview[:35] + "...") if preview and len(preview) > 35 else (preview or "")
   119→            tool_emojis = {
   120→                "terminal": "💻", "web_search": "🔍", "web_extract": "📄",
   121→                "read_file": "📖", "write_file": "✍️", "patch": "🔧",
   122→                "search_files": "🔎", "list_directory": "📂",
   123→                "browser_navigate": "🌐", "browser_click": "👆",
   124→                "text_to_speech": "🔊", "image_generate": "🎨",
   125→                "vision_analyze": "👁️", "process": "⚙️",
   126→            }
   127→            emoji = tool_emojis.get(tool_name, "⚡")
   128→            line = f" {prefix}├─ {emoji} {tool_name}"
   129→            if short:
   130→                line += f"  \"{short}\""
   131→            try:
   132→                spinner.print_above(line)
   133→            except Exception as e:
   134→                logger.debug("Spinner print_above failed: %s", e)
   135→
   136→        if parent_cb:
   137→            _batch.append(tool_name)
   138→            if len(_batch) >= _BATCH_SIZE:
   139→                summary = ", ".join(_batch)
   140→                try:
   141→                    parent_cb("subagent_progress", f"🔀 {prefix}{summary}")
   142→                except Exception as e:
   143→                    logger.debug("Parent callback failed: %s", e)
   144→                _batch.clear()
   145→
   146→    def _flush():
   147→        """Flush remaining batched tool names to gateway on completion."""
   148→        if parent_cb and _batch:
   149→            summary = ", ".join(_batch)
   150→            try:
   151→                parent_cb("subagent_progress", f"🔀 {prefix}{summary}")
   152→            except Exception as e:
   153→                logger.debug("Parent callback flush failed: %s", e)
   154→            _batch.clear()
   155→
   156→    _callback._flush = _flush
   157→    return _callback
   158→
   159→
   160→def _run_single_child(
   161→    task_index: int,
   162→    goal: str,
   163→    context: Optional[str],
   164→    toolsets: Optional[List[str]],
   165→    model: Optional[str],
   166→    max_iterations: int,
   167→    parent_agent,
   168→    task_count: int = 1,
   169→    # Credential overrides from delegation config (provider:model resolution)
   170→    override_provider: Optional[str] = None,
   171→    override_base_url: Optional[str] = None,
   172→    override_api_key: Optional[str] = None,
   173→    override_api_mode: Optional[str] = None,
   174→) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   175→    """
   176→    Spawn and run a single child agent. Called from within a thread.
   177→    Returns a structured result dict.
   178→
   179→    When override_* params are set (from delegation config), the child uses
   180→    those credentials instead of inheriting from the parent.  This enables
   181→    routing subagents to a different provider:model pair (e.g. cheap/fast
   182→    model on OpenRouter while the parent runs on Nous Portal).
   183→    """
   184→    from run_agent import AIAgent
   185→
   186→    child_start = time.monotonic()
   187→
   188→    # When no explicit toolsets given, inherit from parent's enabled toolsets
   189→    # so disabled tools (e.g. web) don't leak to subagents.
   190→    if toolsets:
   191→        child_toolsets = _strip_blocked_tools(toolsets)
   192→    elif parent_agent and getattr(parent_agent, "enabled_toolsets", None):
   193→        child_toolsets = _strip_blocked_tools(parent_agent.enabled_toolsets)
   194→    else:
   195→        child_toolsets = _strip_blocked_tools(DEFAULT_TOOLSETS)
   196→
   197→    child_prompt = _build_child_system_prompt(goal, context)
   198→
   199→    try:
   200→        # Extract parent's API key so subagents inherit auth (e.g. Nous Portal).
   201→        parent_api_key = getattr(parent_agent, "api_key", None)
   202→        if (not parent_api_key) and hasattr(parent_agent, "_client_kwargs"):
   203→            [REDACTED]("api_key")
   204→
   205→        # Build progress callback to relay tool calls to parent display
   206→        child_progress_cb = _build_child_progress_callback(task_index, parent_agent, task_count)
   207→
   208→        # Share the parent's iteration budget so subagent tool calls
   209→        # count toward the session-wide limit.
   210→        shared_budget = getattr(parent_agent, "iteration_budget", None)
   211→
   212→        # Resolve effective credentials: config override > parent inherit
   213→        effective_model = model or parent_agent.model
   214→        effective_provider = override_provider or getattr(parent_agent, "provider", None)
   215→        effective_base_url = override_base_url or parent_agent.base_url
   216→        [REDACTED] or parent_api_key
   217→        effective_api_mode = override_api_mode or getattr(parent_agent, "api_mode", None)
   218→
   219→        child = AIAgent(
   220→            base_url=effective_base_url,
   221→            [REDACTED],
   222→            model=effective_model,
   223→            provider=effective_provider,
   224→            api_mode=effective_api_mode,
   225→            max_iterations=max_iterations,
   226→            max_tokens=getattr(parent_agent, "max_tokens", None),
   227→            reasoning_config=getattr(parent_agent, "reasoning_config", None),
   228→            prefill_messages=getattr(parent_agent, "prefill_messages", None),
   229→            enabled_toolsets=child_toolsets,
   230→            quiet_mode=True,
   231→            ephemeral_system_prompt=child_prompt,
   232→            log_prefix=f"[subagent-{task_index}]",
   233→            platform=parent_agent.platform,
   234→            skip_context_files=True,
   235→            skip_memory=True,
   236→            clarify_callback=None,
   237→            session_db=getattr(parent_agent, '_session_db', None),
   238→            providers_allowed=parent_agent.providers_allowed,
   239→            providers_ignored=parent_agent.providers_ignored,
   240→            providers_order=parent_agent.providers_order,
   241→            provider_sort=parent_agent.provider_sort,
   242→            tool_progress_callback=child_progress_cb,
   243→            iteration_budget=shared_budget,
   244→        )
   245→
   246→        # Set delegation depth so children can't spawn grandchildren
   247→        child._delegate_depth = getattr(parent_agent, '_delegate_depth', 0) + 1
   248→
   249→        # Register child for interrupt propagation
   250→        if hasattr(parent_agent, '_active_children'):
   251→            parent_agent._active_children.append(child)
   252→
   253→        # Run with stdout/stderr suppressed to prevent interleaved output
   254→        devnull = io.StringIO()
   255→        with contextlib.redirect_stdout(devnull), contextlib.redirect_stderr(devnull):
   256→            result = child.run_conversation(user_message=goal)
   257→
   258→        # Flush any remaining batched progress to gateway
   259→        if child_progress_cb and hasattr(child_progress_cb, '_flush'):
   260→            try:
   261→                child_progress_cb._flush()
   262→            except Exception as e:
   263→                logger.debug("Progress callback flush failed: %s", e)
   264→
   265→        duration = round(time.monotonic() - child_start, 2)
   266→
   267→        summary = result.get("final_response") or ""
   268→        completed = result.get("completed", False)
   269→        interrupted = result.get("interrupted", False)
   270→        api_calls = result.get("api_calls", 0)
   271→
   272→        if interrupted:
   273→            status = "interrupted"
   274→        elif completed and summary:
   275→            status = "completed"
   276→        else:
   277→            status = "failed"
   278→
   279→        # Build tool trace from conversation messages (already in memory).
   280→        # Uses tool_call_id to correctly pair parallel tool calls with results.
   281→        tool_trace: list[Dict[str, Any]] = []
   282→        trace_by_id: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
   283→        messages = result.get("messages") or []
   284→        if isinstance(messages, list):
   285→            for msg in messages:
   286→                if not isinstance(msg, dict):
   287→                    continue
   288→                if msg.get("role") == "assistant":
   289→                    for tc in (msg.get("tool_calls") or []):
   290→                        fn = tc.get("function", {})
   291→                        entry_t = {
   292→                            "tool": fn.get("name", "unknown"),
   293→                            "args_bytes": len(fn.get("arguments", "")),
   294→                        }
   295→                        tool_trace.append(entry_t)
   296→                        tc_id = tc.get("id")
   297→                        if tc_id:
   298→                            trace_by_id[tc_id] = entry_t
   299→                elif msg.get("role") == "tool":
   300→                    content = msg.get("content", "")
   301→                    is_error = bool(
   302→                        content and "error" in content[:80].lower()
   303→                    )
   304→                    result_meta = {
   305→                        "result_bytes": len(content),
   306→                        "status": "error" if is_error else "ok",
   307→                    }
   308→                    # Match by tool_call_id for parallel calls
   309→                    tc_id = msg.get("tool_call_id")
   310→                    target = trace_by_id.get(tc_id) if tc_id else None
   311→                    if target is not None:
   312→                        target.update(result_meta)
   313→                    elif tool_trace:
   314→                        # Fallback for messages without tool_call_id
   315→                        tool_trace[-1].update(result_meta)
   316→
   317→        # Determine exit reason
   318→        if interrupted:
   319→            exit_reason = "interrupted"
   320→        elif completed:
   321→            exit_reason = "completed"
   322→        else:
   323→            exit_reason = "max_iterations"
   324→
   325→        # Extract token counts (safe for mock objects)
   326→        _input_tokens = getattr(child, "session_prompt_tokens", 0)
   327→        _output_tokens = getattr(child, "session_completion_tokens", 0)
   328→        _model = getattr(child, "model", None)
   329→
   330→        entry: Dict[str, Any] = {
   331→            "task_index": task_index,
   332→            "status": status,
   333→            "summary": summary,
   334→            "api_calls": api_calls,
   335→            "duration_seconds": duration,
   336→            "model": _model if isinstance(_model, str) else None,
   337→            "exit_reason": exit_reason,
   338→            "tokens": {
   339→                "input": _input_tokens if isinstance(_input_tokens, (int, float)) else 0,
   340→                "output": _output_tokens if isinstance(_output_tokens, (int, float)) else 0,
   341→            },
   342→            "tool_trace": tool_trace,
   343→        }
   344→        if status == "failed":
   345→            entry["error"] = result.get("error", "Subagent did not produce a response.")
   346→
   347→        return entry
   348→
   349→    except Exception as exc:
   350→        duration = round(time.monotonic() - child_start, 2)
   351→        logging.exception(f"[subagent-{task_index}] failed")
   352→        return {
   353→            "task_index": task_index,
   354→            "status": "error",
   355→            "summary": None,
   356→            "error": str(exc),
   357→            "api_calls": 0,
   358→            "duration_seconds": duration,
   359→        }
   360→
   361→    finally:
   362→        # Unregister child from interrupt propagation
   363→        if hasattr(parent_agent, '_active_children'):
   364→            try:
   365→                parent_agent._active_children.remove(child)
   366→            except (ValueError, UnboundLocalError) as e:
   367→                logger.debug("Could not remove child from active_children: %s", e)
   368→
   369→
   370→def delegate_task(
   371→    goal: Optional[str] = None,
   372→    context: Optional[str] = None,
   373→    toolsets: Optional[List[str]] = None,
   374→    tasks: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None,
   375→    max_iterations: Optional[int] = None,
   376→    model: Optional[str] = None,
   377→    provider: Optional[str] = None,
   378→    parent_agent=None,
   379→) -> str:
   380→    """
   381→    Spawn one or more child agents to handle delegated tasks.
   382→
   383→    Supports two modes:
   384→      - Single: provide goal (+ optional context, toolsets, model, provider)
   385→      - Batch:  provide tasks array [{goal, context, toolsets, model, provider}, ...]
   386→
   387→    Per-task model/provider override the delegation config defaults.
   388→
   389→    Returns JSON with results array, one entry per task.
   390→    """
   391→    if parent_agent is None:
   392→        return json.dumps({"error": "delegate_task requires a parent agent context."})
   393→
   394→    # Depth limit
   395→    depth = getattr(parent_agent, '_delegate_depth', 0)
   396→    if depth >= MAX_DEPTH:
   397→        return json.dumps({
   398→            "error": (
   399→                f"Delegation depth limit reached ({MAX_DEPTH}). "
   400→                "Subagents cannot spawn further subagents."
   401→            )
   402→        })
   403→
   404→    # Load config
   405→    cfg = _load_config()
   406→    default_max_iter = cfg.get("max_iterations", DEFAULT_MAX_ITERATIONS)
   407→    effective_max_iter = max_iterations or default_max_iter
   408→
   409→    # Resolve delegation credentials (provider:model pair).
   410→    # When delegation.provider is configured, this resolves the full credential
   411→    # bundle (base_url, api_key, api_mode) via the same runtime provider system
   412→    # used by CLI/gateway startup.  When unconfigured, returns None values so
   413→    # children inherit from the parent.
   414→    try:
   415→        creds = _resolve_delegation_credentials(cfg, parent_agent)
   416→    except ValueError as exc:
   417→        return json.dumps({"error": str(exc)})
   418→
   419→    # Normalize to task list
   420→    if tasks and isinstance(tasks, list):
   421→        task_list = tasks[:MAX_CONCURRENT_CHILDREN]
   422→    elif goal and isinstance(goal, str) and goal.strip():
   423→        task_list = [{"goal": goal, "context": context, "toolsets": toolsets,
   424→                      "model": model, "provider": provider}]
   425→    else:
   426→        return json.dumps({"error": "Provide either 'goal' (single task) or 'tasks' (batch)."})
   427→
   428→    if not task_list:
   429→        return json.dumps({"error": "No tasks provided."})
   430→
   431→    # Validate each task has a goal
   432→    for i, task in enumerate(task_list):
   433→        if not task.get("goal", "").strip():
   434→            return json.dumps({"error": f"Task {i} is missing a 'goal'."})
   435→
   436→    overall_start = time.monotonic()
   437→    results = []
   438→
   439→    n_tasks = len(task_list)
   440→    # Track goal labels for progress display (truncated for readability)
   441→    task_labels = [t["goal"][:40] for t in task_list]
   442→
   443→    if n_tasks == 1:
   444→        # Single task -- run directly (no thread pool overhead)
   445→        t = task_list[0]
   446→        task_creds = _resolve_task_credentials(t, creds, parent_agent)
   447→        result = _run_single_child(
   448→            task_index=0,
   449→            goal=t["goal"],
   450→            context=t.get("context"),
   451→            toolsets=t.get("toolsets") or toolsets,
   452→            model=task_creds["model"],
   453→            max_iterations=effective_max_iter,
   454→            parent_agent=parent_agent,
   455→            task_count=1,
   456→            override_provider=task_creds["provider"],
   457→            override_base_url=task_creds["base_url"],
   458→            override_api_key=task_creds["api_key"],
   459→            override_api_mode=task_creds["api_mode"],
   460→        )
   461→        results.append(result)
   462→    else:
   463→        # Batch -- run in parallel with per-task progress lines
   464→        completed_count = 0
   465→        spinner_ref = getattr(parent_agent, '_delegate_spinner', None)
   466→
   467→        # Save stdout/stderr before the executor — redirect_stdout in child
   468→        # threads races on sys.stdout and can leave it as devnull permanently.
   469→        _saved_stdout = sys.stdout
   470→        _saved_stderr = sys.stderr
   471→
   472→        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=MAX_CONCURRENT_CHILDREN) as executor:
   473→            futures = {}
   474→            for i, t in enumerate(task_list):
   475→                task_creds = _resolve_task_credentials(t, creds, parent_agent)
   476→                future = executor.submit(
   477→                    _run_single_child,
   478→                    task_index=i,
   479→                    goal=t["goal"],
   480→                    context=t.get("context"),
   481→                    toolsets=t.get("toolsets") or toolsets,
   482→                    model=task_creds["model"],
   483→                    max_iterations=effective_max_iter,
   484→                    parent_agent=parent_agent,
   485→                    task_count=n_tasks,
   486→                    override_provider=task_creds["provider"],
   487→                    override_base_url=task_creds["base_url"],
   488→                    override_api_key=task_creds["api_key"],
   489→                    override_api_mode=task_creds["api_mode"],
   490→                )
   491→                futures[future] = i
   492→
   493→            for future in as_completed(futures):
   494→                try:
   495→                    entry = future.result()
   496→                except Exception as exc:
   497→                    idx = futures[future]
   498→                    entry = {
   499→                        "task_index": idx,
   500→                        "status": "error",
   501→                        "summary": None,
   502→                        "error": str(exc),
   503→                        "api_calls": 0,
   504→                        "duration_seconds": 0,
   505→                    }
   506→                results.append(entry)
   507→                completed_count += 1
   508→
   509→                # Print per-task completion line above the spinner
   510→                idx = entry["task_index"]
   511→                label = task_labels[idx] if idx < len(task_labels) else f"Task {idx}"
   512→                dur = entry.get("duration_seconds", 0)
   513→                status = entry.get("status", "?")
   514→                icon = "✓" if status == "completed" else "✗"
   515→                remaining = n_tasks - completed_count
   516→                completion_line = f"{icon} [{idx+1}/{n_tasks}] {label}  ({dur}s)"
   517→                if spinner_ref:
   518→                    try:
   519→                        spinner_ref.print_above(completion_line)
   520→                    except Exception:
   521→                        print(f"  {completion_line}")
   522→                else:
   523→                    print(f"  {completion_line}")
   524→
   525→                # Update spinner text to show remaining count
   526→                if spinner_ref and remaining > 0:
   527→                    try:
   528→                        spinner_ref.update_text(f"🔀 {remaining} task{'s' if remaining != 1 else ''} remaining")
   529→                    except Exception as e:
   530→                        logger.debug("Spinner update_text failed: %s", e)
   531→
   532→        # Restore stdout/stderr in case redirect_stdout race left them as devnull
   533→        sys.stdout = _saved_stdout
   534→        sys.stderr = _saved_stderr
   535→
   536→        # Sort by task_index so results match input order
   537→        results.sort(key=lambda r: r["task_index"])
   538→
   539→    total_duration = round(time.monotonic() - overall_start, 2)
   540→
   541→    return json.dumps({
   542→        "results": results,
   543→        "total_duration_seconds": total_duration,
   544→    }, ensure_ascii=False)
   545→
   546→
   547→def _resolve_task_credentials(task: dict, default_creds: dict, parent_agent) -> dict:
   548→    """Resolve per-task credentials, falling back to config-level defaults.
   549→
   550→    If the task specifies a provider (and optionally model), resolve fresh
   551→    credentials via the shared :func:`resolve_provider_credentials` helper.
   552→    Otherwise use the default_creds from the delegation config.
   553→    """
   554→    task_provider = (task.get("provider") or "").strip().lower()
   555→    task_model = (task.get("model") or "").strip()
   556→
   557→    if not task_provider:
   558→        # No per-task provider — use config defaults, but allow model override
   559→        result = dict(default_creds)
   560→        if task_model:
   561→            result["model"] = task_model
   562→        return result
   563→
   564→    # Per-task provider specified — resolve credentials
   565→    try:
   566→        from agent.auxiliary_client import resolve_provider_credentials
   567→        creds = resolve_provider_credentials(task_provider, task_model or "")
   568→        if creds is None:
   569→            logger.warning(
   570→                "Per-task provider '%s' not configured, falling back to defaults",
   571→                task_provider,
   572→            )
   573→            return default_creds
   574→
   575→        return {
   576→            "model": creds["model"] or default_creds.get("model"),
   577→            "provider": creds["provider"],
   578→            "base_url": creds["base_url"],
   579→            "api_key": creds["api_key"],
   580→            "api_mode": creds["api_mode"],
   581→        }
   582→    except Exception as exc:
   583→        logger.error("Failed to resolve per-task provider '%s': %s", task_provider, exc)
   584→        return default_creds
   585→
   586→
   587→def _resolve_delegation_credentials(cfg: dict, parent_agent) -> dict:
   588→    """Resolve credentials for subagent delegation.
   589→
   590→    If ``delegation.provider`` is configured, resolves the full credential
   591→    bundle (base_url, api_key, api_mode, provider) via the runtime provider
   592→    system — the same path used by CLI/gateway startup.  This lets subagents
   593→    run on a completely different provider:model pair.
   594→
   595→    If no provider is configured, returns None values so the child inherits
   596→    everything from the parent agent.
   597→
   598→    Raises ValueError with a user-friendly message on credential failure.
   599→    """
   600→    configured_model = cfg.get("model") or None
   601→    configured_provider = cfg.get("provider") or None
   602→
   603→    if not configured_provider:
   604→        # No provider override — child inherits everything from parent
   605→        return {
   606→            "model": configured_model,
   607→            "provider": None,
   608→            "base_url": None,
   609→            "api_key": None,
   610→            "api_mode": None,
   611→        }
   612→
   613→    # Provider is configured — resolve full credentials
   614→    try:
   615→        from hermes_cli.runtime_provider import resolve_runtime_provider
   616→        runtime = resolve_runtime_provider(requested=configured_provider)
   617→    except Exception as exc:
   618→        raise ValueError(
   619→            f"Cannot resolve delegation provider '{configured_provider}': {exc}. "
   620→            f"Check that the provider is configured (API key set, valid provider name). "
   621→            f"Available providers: openrouter, nous, zai, kimi-coding, minimax."
   622→        ) from exc
   623→
   624→    api_key = runtime.get("api_key", "")
   625→    if not api_key:
   626→        raise ValueError(
   627→            f"Delegation provider '{configured_provider}' resolved but has no API key. "
   628→            f"Set the appropriate environment variable or run 'hermes login'."
   629→        )
   630→
   631→    return {
   632→        "model": configured_model,
   633→        "provider": runtime.get("provider"),
   634→        "base_url": runtime.get("base_url"),
   635→        "api_key": api_key,
   636→        "api_mode": runtime.get("api_mode"),
   637→    }
   638→
   639→
   640→def _load_config() -> dict:
   641→    """Load delegation config from CLI_CONFIG or persistent config.
   642→
   643→    Checks the runtime config (cli.py CLI_CONFIG) first, then falls back
   644→    to the persistent config (hermes_cli/config.py load_config()) so that
   645→    ``delegation.model`` / ``delegation.provider`` are picked up regardless
   646→    of the entry point (CLI, gateway, cron).
   647→    """
   648→    try:
   649→        from cli import CLI_CONFIG
   650→        cfg = CLI_CONFIG.get("delegation", {})
   651→        if cfg:
   652→            return cfg
   653→    except Exception:
   654→        pass
   655→    try:
   656→        from hermes_cli.config import load_config
   657→        full = load_config()
   658→        return full.get("delegation", {})
   659→    except Exception:
   660→        return {}
   661→
   662→
   663→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   664→# OpenAI Function-Calling Schema
   665→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   666→
   667→DELEGATE_TASK_SCHEMA = {
   668→    "name": "delegate_task",
   669→    "description": (
   670→        "Spawn one or more subagents to work on tasks in isolated contexts. "
   671→        "Each subagent gets its own conversation, terminal session, and toolset. "
   672→        "Only the final summary is returned -- intermediate tool results "
   673→        "never enter your context window.\n\n"
   674→        "TWO MODES (one of 'goal' or 'tasks' is required):\n"
   675→        "1. Single task: provide 'goal' (+ optional context, toolsets)\n"
   676→        "2. Batch (parallel): provide 'tasks' array with up to 3 items. "
   677→        "All run concurrently and results are returned together.\n\n"
   678→        "WHEN TO USE delegate_task:\n"
   679→        "- Reasoning-heavy subtasks (debugging, code review, research synthesis)\n"
   680→        "- Tasks that would flood your context with intermediate data\n"
   681→        "- Parallel independent workstreams (research A and B simultaneously)\n\n"
   682→        "WHEN NOT TO USE (use these instead):\n"
   683→        "- Mechanical multi-step work with no reasoning needed -> use execute_code\n"
   684→        "- Single tool call -> just call the tool directly\n"
   685→        "- Tasks needing user interaction -> subagents cannot use clarify\n\n"
   686→        "IMPORTANT:\n"
   687→        "- Subagents have NO memory of your conversation. Pass all relevant "
   688→        "info (file paths, error messages, constraints) via the 'context' field.\n"
   689→        "- Subagents CANNOT call: delegate_task, clarify, memory, send_message, "
   690→        "execute_code.\n"
   691→        "- Each subagent gets its own terminal session (separate working directory and state).\n"
   692→        "- Results are always returned as an array, one entry per task."
   693→    ),
   694→    "parameters": {
   695→        "type": "object",
   696→        "properties": {
   697→            "goal": {
   698→                "type": "string",
   699→                "description": (
   700→                    "What the subagent should accomplish. Be specific and "
   701→                    "self-contained -- the subagent knows nothing about your "
   702→                    "conversation history."
   703→                ),
   704→            },
   705→            "context": {
   706→                "type": "string",
   707→                "description": (
   708→                    "Background information the subagent needs: file paths, "
   709→                    "error messages, project structure, constraints. The more "
   710→                    "specific you are, the better the subagent performs."
   711→                ),
   712→            },
   713→            "toolsets": {
   714→                "type": "array",
   715→                "items": {"type": "string"},
   716→                "description": (
   717→                    "Toolsets to enable for this subagent. "
   718→                    "Default: inherits your enabled toolsets. "
   719→                    "Common patterns: ['terminal', 'file'] for code work, "
   720→                    "['web'] for research, ['terminal', 'file', 'web'] for "
   721→                    "full-stack tasks."
   722→                ),
   723→            },
   724→            "tasks": {
   725→                "type": "array",
   726→                "items": {
   727→                    "type": "object",
   728→                    "properties": {
   729→                        "goal": {"type": "string", "description": "Task goal"},
   730→                        "context": {"type": "string", "description": "Task-specific context"},
   731→                        "toolsets": {
   732→                            "type": "array",
   733→                            "items": {"type": "string"},
   734→                            "description": "Toolsets for this specific task",
   735→                        },
   736→                        "model": {"type": "string", "description": "Model ID override for this task"},
   737→                        "provider": {"type": "string", "description": "Provider override for this task"},
   738→                    },
   739→                    "required": ["goal"],
   740→                },
   741→                "maxItems": 3,
   742→                "description": (
   743→                    "Batch mode: up to 3 tasks to run in parallel. Each gets "
   744→                    "its own subagent with isolated context and terminal session. "
   745→                    "When provided, top-level goal/context/toolsets are ignored."
   746→                ),
   747→            },
   748→            "model": {
   749→                "type": "string",
   750→                "description": (
   751→                    "Model ID for this subagent. Overrides the delegation config default. "
   752→                    "E.g. 'anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6'. Requires 'provider' to be set."
   753→                ),
   754→            },
   755→            "provider": {
   756→                "type": "string",
   757→                "description": (
   758→                    "Provider for this subagent. Overrides the delegation config default. "
   759→                    "E.g. 'openrouter', 'nous', 'anthropic'. Use switch_model(action='list') "
   760→                    "to see available providers."
   761→                ),
   762→            },
   763→            "max_iterations": {
   764→                "type": "integer",
   765→                "description": (
   766→                    "Max tool-calling turns per subagent (default: 50). "
   767→                    "Only set lower for simple tasks."
   768→                ),
   769→            },
   770→        },
   771→        "required": [],
   772→    },
   773→}
   774→
   775→
   776→# --- Registry ---
   777→from tools.registry import registry
   778→
   779→registry.register(
   780→    name="delegate_task",
   781→    toolset="delegation",
   782→    schema=DELEGATE_TASK_SCHEMA,
   783→    handler=lambda args, **kw: delegate_task(
   784→        goal=args.get("goal"),
   785→        context=args.get("context"),
   786→        toolsets=args.get("toolsets"),
   787→        tasks=args.get("tasks"),
   788→        max_iterations=args.get("max_iterations"),
   789→        model=args.get("model"),
   790→        provider=args.get("provider"),
   791→        parent_agent=kw.get("parent_agent")),
   792→    check_fn=check_delegate_requirements,
   793→)

78→# Codex fallback: uses the Responses API (the only endpoint the Codex
    79→# OAuth token can access) with a fast model for auxiliary tasks.
    80→_CODEX_AUX_MODEL = "gpt-5.3-codex"
    81→_CODEX_AUX_BASE_URL = "https://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex"
    82→
    83→
    84→# ── Codex Responses → chat.completions adapter ─────────────────────────────
    85→# All auxiliary consumers call client.chat.completions.create(**kwargs) and
    86→# read response.choices[0].message.content. This adapter translates those
    87→# calls to the Codex Responses API so callers don't need any changes.
    88→
    89→
    90→def _convert_content_for_responses(content: Any) -> Any:
    91→    """Convert chat.completions content to Responses API format.
    92→
    93→    chat.completions uses:
    94→      {"type": "text", "text": "..."}
    95→      {"type": "image_url", "image_url": {"url": "data:image/png;base64,..."}}
    96→
    97→    Responses API uses:
    98→      {"type": "input_text", "text": "..."}
    99→      {"type": "input_image", "image_url": "data:image/png;base64,..."}
   100→
   101→    If content is a plain string, it's returned as-is (the Responses API
   102→    accepts strings directly for text-only messages).
   103→    """
   104→    if isinstance(content, str):
   105→        return content
   106→    if not isinstance(content, list):
   107→        return str(content) if content else ""
   108→
   109→    converted: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
   110→    for part in content:
   111→        if not isinstance(part, dict):
   112→            continue
   113→        ptype = part.get("type", "")
   114→        if ptype == "text":
   115→            converted.append({"type": "input_text", "text": part.get("text", "")})
   116→        elif ptype == "image_url":
   117→            # chat.completions nests the URL: {"image_url": {"url": "..."}}
   118→            image_data = part.get("image_url", {})
   119→            url = image_data.get("url", "") if isinstance(image_data, dict) else str(image_data)
   120→            entry: Dict[str, Any] = {"type": "input_image", "image_url": url}
   121→            # Preserve detail if specified
   122→            detail = image_data.get("detail") if isinstance(image_data, dict) else None
   123→            if detail:
   124→                entry["detail"] = detail
   125→            converted.append(entry)
   126→        elif ptype in ("input_text", "input_image"):
   127→            # Already in Responses format — pass through
   128→            converted.append(part)
   129→        else:
   130→            # Unknown content type — try to preserve as text
   131→            text = part.get("text", "")
   132→            if text:
   133→                converted.append({"type": "input_text", "text": text})
   134→
   135→    return converted or ""
   136→
   137→
   138→class _CodexCompletionsAdapter:
   139→    """Drop-in shim that accepts chat.completions.create() kwargs and
   140→    routes them through the Codex Responses streaming API."""
   141→
   142→    def __init__(self, real_client: OpenAI, model: str):
   143→        self._client = real_client
   144→        self._model = model
   145→
   146→    def create(self, **kwargs) -> Any:
   147→        messages = kwargs.get("messages", [])
   148→        model = kwargs.get("model", self._model)
   149→        temperature = kwargs.get("temperature")
   150→
   151→        # Separate system/instructions from conversation messages.
   152→        # Convert chat.completions multimodal content blocks to Responses
   153→        # API format (input_text / input_image instead of text / image_url).
   154→        instructions = "You are a helpful assistant."
   155→        input_msgs: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
   156→        for msg in messages:
   157→            role = msg.get("role", "user")
   158→            content = msg.get("content") or ""
   159→            if role == "system":
   160→                instructions = content if isinstance(content, str) else str(content)
   161→            else:
   162→                input_msgs.append({
   163→                    "role": role,
   164→                    "content": _convert_content_for_responses(content),
   165→                })
   166→
   167→        resp_kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {
   168→            "model": model,
   169→            "instructions": instructions,
   170→            "input": input_msgs or [{"role": "user", "content": ""}],
   171→            "store": False,
   172→        }
   173→
   174→        # Note: the Codex endpoint (chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex) does NOT
   175→        # support max_output_tokens or temperature — omit to avoid 400 errors.
   176→
   177→        # Tools support for flush_memories and similar callers
   178→        tools = kwargs.get("tools")
   179→        if tools:
   180→            converted = []
   181→            for t in tools:
   182→                fn = t.get("function", {}) if isinstance(t, dict) else {}
   183→                name = fn.get("name")
   184→                if not name:
   185→                    continue
   186→                converted.append({
   187→                    "type": "function",
   188→                    "name": name,
   189→                    "description": fn.get("description", ""),
   190→                    "parameters": fn.get("parameters", {}),
   191→                })
   192→            if converted:
   193→                resp_kwargs["tools"] = converted
   194→
   195→        # Stream and collect the response
   196→        text_parts: List[str] = []
   197→        tool_calls_raw: List[Any] = []
   198→        usage = None
   199→
   200→        try:
   201→            with self._client.responses.stream(**resp_kwargs) as stream:
   202→                for _event in stream:
   203→                    pass
   204→                final = stream.get_final_response()
   205→
   206→            # Extract text and tool calls from the Responses output
   207→            for item in getattr(final, "output", []):
   208→                item_type = getattr(item, "type", None)
   209→                if item_type == "message":
   210→                    for part in getattr(item, "content", []):
   211→                        ptype = getattr(part, "type", None)
   212→                        if ptype in ("output_text", "text"):
   213→                            text_parts.append(getattr(part, "text", ""))
   214→                elif item_type == "function_call":
   215→                    tool_calls_raw.append(SimpleNamespace(
   216→                        id=getattr(item, "call_id", ""),
   217→                        type="function",
   218→                        function=SimpleNamespace(
   219→                            name=getattr(item, "name", ""),
   220→                            arguments=getattr(item, "arguments", "{}"),
   221→                        ),
   222→                    ))
   223→
   224→            resp_usage = getattr(final, "usage", None)
   225→            if resp_usage:
   226→                usage = SimpleNamespace(
   227→                    prompt_tokens=getattr(resp_usage, "input_tokens", 0),
   228→                    completion_tokens=getattr(resp_usage, "output_tokens", 0),
   229→                    total_tokens=getattr(resp_usage, "total_tokens", 0),
   230→                )
   231→        except Exception as exc:
   232→            logger.debug("Codex auxiliary Responses API call failed: %s", exc)
   233→            raise
   234→
   235→        content = "".join(text_parts).strip() or None
   236→
   237→        # Build a response that looks like chat.completions
   238→        message = SimpleNamespace(
   239→            role="assistant",
   240→            content=content,
   241→            tool_calls=tool_calls_raw or None,
   242→        )
   243→        choice = SimpleNamespace(
   244→            index=0,
   245→            message=message,
   246→            finish_reason="stop" if not tool_calls_raw else "tool_calls",
   247→        )
   248→        return SimpleNamespace(
   249→            choices=[choice],
   250→            model=model,
   251→            usage=usage,
   252→        )
   253→
   254→
   255→class _CodexChatShim:
   256→    """Wraps the adapter to provide client.chat.completions.create()."""
   257→

   261→
   262→class CodexAuxiliaryClient:
   263→    """OpenAI-client-compatible wrapper that routes through Codex Responses API.
   264→
   265→    Consumers can call client.chat.completions.create(**kwargs) as normal.
   266→    Also exposes .api_key and .base_url for introspection by async wrappers.
   267→    """
   268→
   269→    def __init__(self, real_client: OpenAI, model: str):
   270→        self._real_client = real_client
   271→        adapter = _CodexCompletionsAdapter(real_client, model)
   272→        self.chat = _CodexChatShim(adapter)
   273→        self.[REDACTED]
   274→        self.base_url = real_client.base_url
   275→
   276→    def close(self):
   277→        self._real_client.close()

   280→class _AsyncCodexCompletionsAdapter:
   281→    """Async version of the Codex Responses adapter.
   282→
   283→    Wraps the sync adapter via asyncio.to_thread() so async consumers
   284→    (web_tools, session_search) can await it as normal.
   285→    """
   286→
   287→    def __init__(self, sync_adapter: _CodexCompletionsAdapter):
   288→        self._sync = sync_adapter
   289→
   290→    async def create(self, **kwargs) -> Any:
   291→        import asyncio
   292→        return await asyncio.to_thread(self._sync.create, **kwargs)
   293→
   294→
   295→class _AsyncCodexChatShim:
   296→    def __init__(self, adapter: _AsyncCodexCompletionsAdapter):
   297→        self.completions = adapter
   298→
   299→
   300→class AsyncCodexAuxiliaryClient:
   301→    """Async-compatible wrapper matching AsyncOpenAI.chat.completions.create()."""
   302→
   303→    def __init__(self, sync_wrapper: "CodexAuxiliaryClient"):
   304→        sync_adapter = sync_wrapper.chat.completions
   305→        async_adapter = _AsyncCodexCompletionsAdapter(sync_adapter)
   306→        self.chat = _AsyncCodexChatShim(async_adapter)
   307→        self.[REDACTED]
   308→        self.base_url = sync_wrapper.base_url

   310→
   311→def _read_nous_auth() -> Optional[dict]:
   312→    """Read and validate ~/.hermes/auth.json for an active Nous provider.
   313→
   314→    Returns the provider state dict if Nous is active with tokens,
   315→    otherwise None.
   316→    """
   317→    try:
   318→        if not _AUTH_JSON_PATH.is_file():
   319→            return None
   320→        data = json.loads(_AUTH_JSON_PATH.read_text())
   321→        if data.get("active_provider") != "nous":
   322→            return None
   323→        provider = data.get("providers", {}).get("nous", {})
   324→        # Must have at least an access_token or agent_key
   325→        if not provider.get("agent_key") and not provider.get("access_token"):
   326→            return None
   327→        return provider

343→def _read_codex_access_token() -> Optional[str]:
   344→    """Read a valid Codex OAuth access token from Hermes auth store (~/.hermes/auth.json)."""
   345→    try:
   346→        from hermes_cli.auth import _read_codex_tokens
   347→        data = _read_codex_tokens()
   348→        tokens = data.get("tokens", {})
   349→        access_token = tokens.get("access_token")
   350→        if isinstance(access_token, str) and access_token.strip():
   351→            return access_token.strip()
   352→        return None
   353→    except Exception as exc:
   354→        logger.debug("Could not read Codex auth for auxiliary client: %s", exc)
   355→        return None
   356→
   357→
   358→def _resolve_api_key_provider() -> Tuple[Optional[OpenAI], Optional[str]]:
   359→    """Try each API-key provider in PROVIDER_REGISTRY order.
   360→
   361→    Returns (client, model) for the first provider whose env var is set,
   362→    or (None, None) if none are configured.
   363→    """
   364→    try:
   365→        from hermes_cli.auth import PROVIDER_REGISTRY
   366→    except ImportError:
   367→        logger.debug("Could not import PROVIDER_REGISTRY for API-key fallback")
   368→        return None, None
   369→
   370→    for provider_id, pconfig in PROVIDER_REGISTRY.items():
   371→        if pconfig.auth_type != "api_key":
   372→            continue
   373→        # Check if any of the provider's env vars are set
   374→        api_key = ""
   375→        for env_var in pconfig.api_key_env_vars:
   376→            val = os.getenv(env_var, "").strip()
   377→            if val:
   378→                api_key = val
   379→                break
   380→        if not api_key:
   381→            continue
   382→        # Resolve base URL (with optional env-var override)
   383→        # Kimi Code keys (sk-kimi-) need api.kimi.com/coding/v1
   384→        env_url = ""
   385→        if pconfig.base_url_env_var:
   386→            env_url = os.getenv(pconfig.base_url_env_var, "").strip()
   387→        if env_url:
   388→            base_url = env_url.rstrip("/")
   389→        elif provider_id == "kimi-coding" and api_key.startswith("sk-kimi-"):
   390→            base_url = "https://api.kimi.com/coding/v1"
   391→        else:
   392→            base_url = pconfig.inference_base_url
   393→        model = _API_KEY_PROVIDER_AUX_MODELS.get(provider_id, "default")
   394→        logger.debug("Auxiliary text client: %s (%s)", pconfig.name, model)
   395→        extra = {}
   396→        if "api.kimi.com" in base_url.lower():
   397→            extra["default_headers"] = {"User-Agent": "KimiCLI/1.0"}
   398→        return OpenAI(api_key=api_key, base_url=base_url, **extra), model
   399→
   400→    return None, None
   401→
   402→
   403→# ── Provider resolution helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────
   404→
   405→def _get_auxiliary_provider(task: str = "") -> str:
   406→    """Read the provider override for a specific auxiliary task.
   407→
   408→    Checks AUXILIARY_{TASK}_PROVIDER first (e.g. AUXILIARY_VISION_PROVIDER),
   409→    then CONTEXT_{TASK}_PROVIDER (for the compression section's summary_provider),
   410→    then falls back to "auto".  Returns one of: "auto", "openrouter", "nous", "main".
   411→    """
   412→    if task:
   413→        for prefix in ("AUXILIARY_", "CONTEXT_"):
   414→            val = os.getenv(f"{prefix}{task.upper()}_PROVIDER", "").strip().lower()
   415→            if val and val != "auto":
   416→                return val
   417→    return "auto"
   418→
   419→
   420→def _try_openrouter() -> Tuple[Optional[OpenAI], Optional[str]]:
   421→    or_key = os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY")
   422→    if not or_key:
   423→        return None, None
   424→    logger.debug("Auxiliary client: OpenRouter")
   425→    return OpenAI(api_key=or_key, base_url=OPENROUTER_BASE_URL,
   426→                   default_headers=_OR_HEADERS), _OPENROUTER_MODEL
   427→

   429→def _try_nous() -> Tuple[Optional[OpenAI], Optional[str]]:
   430→    nous = _read_nous_auth()
   431→    if not nous:
   432→        return None, None
   433→    global auxiliary_is_nous
   434→    auxiliary_is_nous = True
   435→    logger.debug("Auxiliary client: Nous Portal")
   436→    return (
   437→        OpenAI(api_key=_nous_api_key(nous), base_url=_nous_base_url()),
   438→        _NOUS_MODEL,
   439→    )
   440→
   441→
   442→def _try_custom_endpoint() -> Tuple[Optional[OpenAI], Optional[str]]:
   443→    custom_base = os.getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL")
   444→    custom_key = os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY")
   445→    if not custom_base or not custom_key:
   446→        return None, None
   447→    model = os.getenv("OPENAI_MODEL") or "gpt-4o-mini"
   448→    logger.debug("Auxiliary client: custom endpoint (%s)", model)
   449→    return OpenAI(api_key=custom_key, base_url=custom_base), model
   450→
   451→
   452→def _try_codex() -> Tuple[Optional[Any], Optional[str]]:
   453→    [REDACTED]()
   454→    if not codex_token:
   455→        return None, None
   456→    logger.debug("Auxiliary client: Codex OAuth (%s via Responses API)", _CODEX_AUX_MODEL)
   457→    real_client = OpenAI(api_key=codex_token, base_url=_CODEX_AUX_BASE_URL)
   458→    return CodexAuxiliaryClient(real_client, _CODEX_AUX_MODEL), _CODEX_AUX_MODEL
   459→
   460→
   461→def _resolve_forced_provider(forced: str) -> Tuple[Optional[OpenAI], Optional[str]]:
   462→    """Resolve a specific forced provider.  Returns (None, None) if creds missing."""
   463→    if forced == "openrouter":
   464→        client, model = _try_openrouter()
   465→        if client is None:
   466→            logger.warning("auxiliary.provider=openrouter but OPENROUTER_API_KEY not set")
   467→        return client, model
   468→
   469→    if forced == "nous":
   470→        client, model = _try_nous()
   471→        if client is None:
   472→            logger.warning("auxiliary.provider=nous but Nous Portal not configured (run: hermes login)")
   473→        return client, model
   474→
   475→    if forced == "codex":
   476→        client, model = _try_codex()
   477→        if client is None:
   478→            logger.warning("auxiliary.provider=codex but no Codex OAuth token found (run: hermes model)")
   479→        return client, model
   480→
   481→    if forced == "main":
   482→        # "main" = skip OpenRouter/Nous, use the main chat model's credentials.
   483→        for try_fn in (_try_custom_endpoint, _try_codex, _resolve_api_key_provider):
   484→            client, model = try_fn()
   485→            if client is not None:
   486→                return client, model
   487→        logger.warning("auxiliary.provider=main but no main endpoint credentials found")
   488→        return None, None
   489→
   490→    # Unknown provider name — fall through to auto
   491→    logger.warning("Unknown auxiliary.provider=%r, falling back to auto", forced)
   492→    return None, None
